Conclusion 

THIS article applies to little boys as well as to little girls, even though one has to be more discreet in the use of colors for Sonny. Teaching children the colors that are best for them and which to avoid, as well as how to affect these colors in all their varying shades through their wardrobe, cannot but prove of inestimable value to them later in life. When they know the fundamentals of color selection they can make experiments and variations of their own accord. 

As they grow older color and type possibilities become more intricate, but with these suggestions as a base, the subtle uses of color and shades can hold no terrors and no pitfalls for them. 176

Types and Colors for Tiny Tots 

Now you have made out a color chart for yourself. Why not make out one for each child, consulting her the while?

It is never too soon to make your daughter color-conscious. 

Have her make out the chart, and have her consult you. 

She can do it in the form of a game, and in this way learn basic color knowledge that she can improve upon and experiment with as she grows up. Teaching her this now will save her and yourself many dollars and the making of those tragic mistakes in harmony and good taste that are so often seen. 177

Chapter IX YOU AND YOUR LINES 

IT is taken for granted that we dress for protection from the elements. Actually we dress to be ornamental. Every woman knows, or should know, that by employing certain lines, certain angles, certain twists of materials in her clothing, she can so change her contours as to make them appear perfectly symmetrical and graceful, even if they are not. Also we should realize that with our clothing we can exaggerate and distort our lovely figures beyond recognition to-day, even as in those horrible days of the bustle and the leg o'mutton sleeve, although of course not quite so obviously. 

Therefore, every lovely lady must know what lines are, where to find them, and how to use them. Understand that 178

You and Your Lines this has absolutely nothing to do with style or style changes. Our figures do not change with our changing conceptions of style. And for the next hundred years or so, I am sure, we will see only improvement in our style ideas, all based upon our present conception of loveliness. 

The Twentieth Century idea of beauty, as it is expressed not only in the mode but in our architecture, our decorations, our sculpture, and our painting, depends on simplicity for its effect. It is based on the simple geometric figures, the line, the circle, and the triangle. These figures, which are pleasing because of their simplicity, can be found in every object about us and are perfectly expressed in the human figure. I really believe that good style has now found its goal, and though there may be a few sporadic fads, the principles of good style will not change for ever so long.

Make Your Clothing Im prove Your

Appearance 

WITH just a little study and attention to the matter, the lines in clothing can do about anything you wish them to do, even alter and improve the appearance of your figure. Since you must wear this clothing why not select it intelligently?

A line is anything, any detail in the draping of our figures with clothing, that arrests the eye. We have innumerable lines in our clothing. Remember that the eyes are lazy things, and that they prefer to follow lines in the sweep that the lines indicate rather than to figure out just what is under that line and where that line is going. That 179

Lovely Ladies is the very reason that you can, with the lines in your clothing, make your figure seem anything that you wish it to seem, correct its faults, emphasize its good points. 

Lines 

Now let's find out what all the possible The Different lines are from lovely crown to dainty foot, even though all these lines may not be in the ensemble all at once. First, there are the lines around the face, which are hat lines and neck lines. Next is the waist line, which we can nowadays place almost anywhere we wish to place it, and wherever it is most becoming to our figures, regardless of nature's intention. 

Possibly between your neck line and your waist line will be lines such as edges of boleros, bands of tucks or pleats, or braids, or embroidery, appliqué, or printed designs in the fabric. Whether these lines go up and down or from right to left makes all the difference in how long or how short you seem between the neck line and the waist line. More of that later.

After the waist line we have, usually, the hem of one's skirt, than which there is no more drastic way to cut off the figure, and with which nine women out of ten commit the worst sartorial crimes in the name of style. When will we have the courage to regulate the length of our skirts to suit ourselves instead of the mode? Well, a lovely lady does, anyhow, always!

Possibly between the waist line and the hem you have some other lines such as I have described between the neck line and the waist, made by the manipulations of 180

You and Your Lines needle, thread, and fabric. One particular line that you may find here is the hem of a jacket, edge of a blouse or tunic. These are horizontal lines, or left-to-right lines, and cut your figure in a way that shortens it and broadens it. I will explain in detail later. Or, if vertical lines occur between waist line and hem, such as vertical panels, tucks, or hanging pleats, these will lengthen your appearance from the waist down and consequently slenderize it. 

And now that hem. Be merciless with yourself and consider your mirror your best friend. Either you have attractive limbs or you have not-there is no middle ground. If you have not, why should you insist upon proclaiming this fact to the world, in this day and age when intelligent women are no longer slaves to fashion? Not, of course, that I am advising you to wear long skirts if everyone else is wearing short ones at the moment. But you can always stop your hem at a place that gives your whole figure a better line. The difference of just an inch of material will not only alter the appearance of your-let me say itlegs, but your whole silhouette.

The same thing of course applies to the lines you will find from your shoulder to your wrist.

All of these lines you will find in your clothing, and these lines you can employ to your own advantage or foolishly disregard to your disadvantage.

LINES going from top to bottom, or from down to up, as the children say, are called "vertical lines." These are the lines that lengthen your silhouette. These are the 181

Lovely Ladies lines that, by lengthening your silhouette, make you appear taller and thinner. These are the lines to be avoided by the tall, slender women and by short, slender women. And these are the lines to be sought after by women who wish to appear more slender.

How Lines Aid or Mar the Figure

The other type of lines are those that go from left to right, called "horizontal lines." These lines cut your figure in half, or quarter, whichever the case may be, and make it shorter. These lines make your figure look stouter, and these lines are to be scrupulously avoided by the woman who wishes to achieve tallness and slenderness in her ensemble. 

So there you are! Remember that the line is quicker than the eye-that eyes follow lines, and thus one gets an impression that is not actually correct, but an impression just the same. Not only must you be careful of the lines you select in your clothing in order to employ them to your best advantage, but you must take care not to disguise the beauty you already have.

Lines That 

Frame the 

Face 

PERHAPS these are the most important lines. Lines around your face, that either frame it correctly and bring out all its natural charms, or hide them and distort your face certainly they are very important.

First one must realize what these lines are. They are the lines of your hat, your hair lines and your neck lines. 

First-hat lines. Hat lines are the top of the crown, the 182

You and Your Lines band of ribbon or trimming around the crown, the brim, the edge of the brim, and the trimming of the hat generally. A hat either has a side-to-side, hence broadening and shortening movement, or it has a down-to-up movement, which makes your face and neck and whole silhouette longer, taller, and thinner. Or a hat cleverly combines the two in a way to offset either tendency. 

Neck lines next. These are badly used so often! A neck line either shortens and broadens your neck and face, or lengthens and slenderizes it. Naturally, if you have a round face you should use the neck lines that lengthen and slenderize it, and if your face is a bit too thin and your neck long and thin you should avoid these neck lines.

The coiffure. Remember that the area of your skin exposed between the edge of your hair and the edge of your neck line is the high light on your portrait of yourself; and the more you lengthen this the more slender your face and neck will seem. Therefore, if you have a small face, or a short and plump face, you may wear your hair as high off your forehead as you can, and as close to the ears and cheeks as you can. Low on the forehead and fluffed out around your face, your hair only serves to shorten your face and make it seem that much chubbier. 

Simply reverse the order, if your face is too thin.

Lower and softer on the forehead goes your hair, wider and softer around your cheeks. It is miraculous the way you can alter your appearance with your coiffure.

There are in general three types of faces, with of course variations which you can ascertain for yourself. Learn 183

Lovely Ladies what general type of face you have and the variations will take care of themselves.

These three general types of faces are: Round, Square, Thin-and-Long.

The Round Face 

A ROUND face is usually accompanied by a rather short, chubby neck, and of course is usually on a short little person. Whether you are short and chubby or not, if you have a round face that needs to be lengthened, and a short neck that needs to be lengthened, you can do this by adjusting the lines around your face.

Picture three round-faced little ladies who smile at you, all in a row. One of them is all wrong. With a total disregard for the fitness of things, she wears a perfectly round hat with a short round crown and an all-round brim—a brim that curves up and off her face. And as though this were not bad enough, her hat shows a choux of feathers that stick straight out at the side (horizontal lines!) and make her face seem broader than it already is, which is too round-broad for beauty. And she would wear her hair fluffed out around her face, broadening it yet again. Then the poor dear lady, despite the fact that she simply hates to look so chubby and chunky, is wearing a round neck line, the only neck line that shortens and broadens the neck and face.

A woman with a round face, or a plump face, especially if she has a short neck as well, should never wear round lines around her face; neither round neck lines, nor high 184

You and Your Lines collars, nor choker beads, nor round hat crowns, nor round hat brims, particularly if they are close to her head, as in the turban shapes. 

What she should do is wear always high and square crowns, a rather low and always V or square or a deep oval neckline; hair high off her forehead and close to her cheeks and ears at the side; and hat lines and ornaments that have an up-and-out movement, never a down and curved and drooping movement. 

Oh, yes, I forgot to mention the fluffy feather or fur neck piece which women with round faces and short necks, and especially those with a high bust, seem to love to affect. Rather wear longish tuxedo effects in your scarfs and flat furs that fall in longish effects. 

Thin, Long

Faces SO MANY Women, especially because of their efforts to preserve a slender figure, have thin long faces and long necks. These can be made to look gracefully slender with the lines you employ around your face, or simply cadaverous. You must wear all lines that do not add to the length of your face and neck, but instead, give it breadth, and consequently softness and plumpness.

You will do all of the things I told our plump-faced sister not to do-wear your hair low on your forehead, fluffed and softened around your face; avoid V neck lines as you would the plague, and affect round ones and not too low oval ones, as well as high collars. Your hats should never have an up-and-out movement; all their crowns will 185

Lovely Ladies be round and close fitting, all their brims will shoot downward, if they have any brim, all their trimming will go side-out, never up. In short, you will affect all the horizontal lines in those you use around your face and avoid all vertical ones. And you will never wear long, hanging scarfs, or jabots, or long strings of beads. These have vertical lines that will lengthen your face and neck. Furs and feathers are yours to soften your face and fill in the long sweep of your neck at the back.

Square Heads and Square Jaws persons with

PEOPLE are like that-sometimes. It is an erroneous idea that all square jaws are stubborn. But if you refuse to soften and make the contours of your face, neck, and shoulders more round and feminine, yes, then I will have to admit you are a "square-head"! 

But if you soften those lines, wear neck lines that make your broad, square shoulders slope, avoid square neck lines and square hat crowns, you will be another person entirely.

Often the sweetest disposition in the world is camouflaged in a square face with a seemingly stubborn jaw.

Round lines and softness around your face will do the trick. Your hair, for instance, should not necessarily lie low on your forehead-really a bit high would be better, and although not fluffed out around your cheeks, which would broaden your face, certainly it should be soft around your face and be brought low in the back behind your ears. Your hats should be softly curved and show enough 186

You and Your Lines brim not to make your face seem more out-jutting than the hat, your neck lines round or oval, and medium low, never square, and usually not V. The neck line, too, should be cut longer on the shoulder than usual, farther from the neck, that is. This narrows your broad shoulder marvelously.

Be careful not to broaden your face in your effort to give it rounder, softer lines. You can still give it round lines without fluffing your hair out too much at the sides, as you would for the thin face, which must be both broadened and softened. Your face has to be softened, not broadened. Slenderizing

Lines 

SUPPOSE we go on and find out what all the slenderizing lines are, where they are found, and how to use them. Later there will be some special corrective line hints. We have already discussed the slenderizing lines found around the face in the paragraph devoted to the little lady with the chubby face and the short neck. Now we will find the other slenderizing lines. 

Dressing to achieve slenderness in your appearance is an art. It can be done, too. Starving to acquire an unnatural slenderness is a dead fad, as harmful as the one of compressing the waist into the space of as few inches as possible, and as stupid as the Chinese idea of breaking and binding the feet of women in the name of beauty. 

We have at last adopted a conception of beauty as interpreted in the female figure similar to the artistic concep- 187

Lovely Ladies tion of the Greeks. And we have no reason to expect that this idea will merge into anything else for centuries. 

But we do not admire embonpoint, and never will! 

We admire simply the rounded and well-proportioned feminine figure, which is well set off by clothes in lines that give it a graceful slenderness without in any way distorting or binding or compressing it.

After the hat, hair, and neck lines, we have the waist line, and some lines between the neck line and waist, perhaps. The lines which are not slenderizing, and consequently to be avoided, are the left-to-right lines, or horizontal lines. What are they? Well, bands of embroidery, for instance, or bands of trimming of any kindanything, in fact, that decidedly and too obviously goes from left to right, between your neck line and your waist line, is to be avoided by anyone desiring a slenderizing effect of the upper part of the figure. 

Long hanging ties, scarfs, jabots, trimming of any kind that goes from up to down, or from neck line to waist, is very desirable indeed! It slenderizes the figure, lengthens it, and makes it seem less broad. 

For the woman with a very large bust, the wide V neck line filled in with a contrasting colored vestee is ideal. 

The edge of a bolero, or a short jacket, is another bad line to be avoided by one looking for slenderizing effects. 

Then the belt and its placing, and the decision whether it is to be an inch wide or ten inches wide. Any belt, no matter how narrow, cuts the figure. If you are very heavy indeed never wear any belt at all, or if you do, 188

You and Your Lines only a little belt at the back. And if you wish to achieve a slenderizing effect in your silhouette never wear a broad belt. The most unthinking woman should see that a wide belt or girdle swathing the hips, if the hips are broad, is very ugly.

Below this belt one encounters again horizontal lines that are unflattering to the large woman, lines that shorten the figure and make it heavier. These are the edges of blouses and tunics, the edges of jackets (particularly if they are fur-trimmed), bands of embroidery, tiers, pleats, tucks. Any line going from left to right, anywhere on the figure, is not a slenderizing line. Avoid as many of them as you possibly can if you wish to achieve slenderness with your clothing. As nearly as you possibly can, give your body one long sweep from neck line to hem, and then have this neck line a pointed V, the hem irregular, with hanging, pointed tabs and panels below it. 

Skirt lines we have already mentioned. The shorter the hem of your gown the shorter it will make you look, and the less slender. The truly slenderizing hem line is the irregular one. Select now the best hem line for your figure. 

A woman who is correctly and attractively dressed is always comme au fait.

Below the skirt hem you have very little in the way of slenderizing or unslenderizing lines save in your shoe straps. Certainly straps on your pumps will make your feet seem shorter, and your ankles shorter and heavier, by cutting the long sweep from the hem of your skirt to the toe of your shoe. Opera pumps are the most slender- 189

Lovely Ladies izing, as are V-shaped heels on your stockings, and shoes that show two colors of leather, or leather or trimming broken up in an artistic arrangement of colors and pieces. 

A large woman should wear neatly fitting, neither too tight nor too baggy clothes, and one-piece effects only, never two-piece effects, particularly if they show two decided color contrasts, as a dark skirt and a lighter blouse. 

It is all a matter of the eye, of course. Lines do not actually add or detract one pound from the figure, but they can and do give the effect of shortening, broadening, or slenderizing the figure. The next time you are on a train, notice that the unfenced lots seem larger than those of the same size that are fenced. This is because the eye is lazy and travels the way anything tempts it to travel, anything in the way of a line, that is. If the lines of a costume suggest that your eye travel from head to foot in a straight unbroken sweep you will think that the object you have just observed is taller and more slender than it is. Every arresting line going from left to right that your eye has noted will add just that much breadth to the message conveyed to the brain. 

Special Hints for Tall

Women 

SUPPOSE that you are very tall and probably very thin. You certainly are not interested in anything that will make you the least bit taller or thinner! You will dress to minimize your height and make your tall slenderness a part of your personality. 190

You and Your Lines 

Generally speaking, you will wear exactly the opposite lines from those we have just outlined and called “slenderizing.” Then there are some other special lines you can employ as well to broaden and fill out your figure, the while you are making it a little shorter here, a little shorter there, a little shorter in the other place. 

First go back to where we explained about the lines that go around your face, and relearn what you should affect in your coiffure, in your hats and neck lines. And then get it into your head, once and forever, that you can never wear one single, solitary vertical line, or up-to-down line, starting with the very tip-top crown of your hat, which must be as close to your head as possible, never high or square, always round, and descending to the brim of your hat, which must never shoot up off your face, and should be neither too small nor too large. The trimming on your hat must also not be upstanding. Your coiffure must be brought low on your forehead and rather out around your cheeks and ears.

Your neck lines must be round or oval, or of the little high-collar variety, and never a V. And not a hanging scarf end, jabot, or tie must you allow yourself; but instead ties and trimmings that go from left to right. Have all of these you can possibly select in one costume. Boleros, rows of tucks or pleats, left-to-right rows of insertion or embroidery, or left-to-right printed designs you must always use between your neck line and waist line to shorten the upper part of your figure.

Then we come to the belt, which must be as wide as is 191

Lovely Ladies becoming to you and placed at just the right angle for your figure, so that it neither shortens your upper figure nor your lower figure too much. But never wear a dress or coat that has no belt and that causes the eye to travel in a long, unbroken sweep from your head to your feet, because this will make you look even taller and thinner than you really are.

I must suggest here that you always affect two-piece garments, preferably of the blouse-and-skirt or skirttunic variety. That gives you another shortening line between your wide belt and the hem of your blouse. 

Now for your skirt. It may surprise you to know that you must at all costs avoid pleated skirts, even though you have always thought that pleated skirts would give you a little of the necessary fullness you felt that you needed. They do, but they also show too many vertical lines and consequently defeat their purpose. A skirt with fulness applied in a circular fashion will be far more successful. And a skirt with a left-to-right tiered effect, whether it be one or two tiers, is ideal. Here again come the possibilities of inserted bands-embroidery, pleats, tucks, designs in the material, and appliqué, that you used in the space between your neck line and your waist line-all going from left to right, and consequently all shortening the figure. 

Where should the tall woman end her skirts? This problem is always a difficult one. If your skirts are too long they lengthen your figure and make you taller, whereas if they are too short they make your figure look ridiculous. 192

You and Your Lines 

You must strike, then, a happy medium. The proper skirt length for a very tall woman is about the middle of the calf, except of course in evening gowns, which are more becoming when they have an irregular hem line. But dips and tabs, hanging panels that fall below your irregular skirt hem are, alas, lengthening to the silhouette, so you must avoid them, no matter how graceful they are. 

And now, your shoes. Remember you are an unusually tall, slender woman, for all of these suggestions do not apply if you are not. And there is one crime that most other tall women commit, but which you, being a lovely lady, would never be guilty of, and that is the crime of trying to minimize your height by drooping or hanging your head. Nothing could have a worse psychological effect upon yourself or upon the people who see you. To be tall is nothing to be ashamed of. It gives you indeed an effectiveness that many a shorter sister will never have, a picturesqueness and dignity which you can have if you handle yourself properly. 

But to get back to your shoes. Now everywhere you can obtain shoes of the dressier variety and evening slippers with flat and Cuban heels. If you really are extra tall, then of course you will want to affect these. Personally, I wouldn't even if I were not just tall but extra tall; I would just dress to minimize this as much as I could but at no sacrifice whatsoever to my modishness and the rest of the world could just take it or leave it. Because of this very attitude they will probably "take" it joyfully! 193

Lovely Ladies 

PERHAPS you are a tiny little thing. Of

Tiny Women course there are certain things that you should do and other things that you need not do. For example, if you are very small and have a consuming desire to impress people with your dignity and personality, you can avoid wearing Peter Pan collars, sweaters and skirts, and all of the other "little girl" styles that really are adorable for you, but not if you don't think So. 

And you can dress to give your figure both height and dignity. The best part about this is that while you are giving your figure height, you will be slenderizing it at the same time. But don't exaggerate this giving of height to your figure. It is quite obviously ridiculous for you to wear exaggeratedly high crowns on your hats, or long, wispy, trailing lines that would only be suitable for your tall, slender sister.

One all-desirable material for you, whenever it is in good taste, is velvet, and black velvet at that, with real lace touches. If this gives you a slightly "Little Lord 

Fauntleroy" look, what of it? There was a time when velvet was allowed only to royalty, and since then it has ever sounded its note of dignity and subtle reproach to frivolity. Regardless of whether velvet is at the moment tout-à-fait de la mode, it is a desirable material for the tiny woman who wishes to achieve more dignity in her clothing. 

The short and plump woman has no problem, since all the lines that add to her height add to her slenderness; 194

You and Your Lines but the tiny and thin woman must be careful of certain things-black, for instance, unlightened with any color touch. It will make her look too like a little sad blackbird.

All tiny women must avoid large designs, large hats, huge gobs of fur trimming.

Small women, then, who wish to achieve individuality and dignity in their clothing, will seek the simple, tailored lines; will avoid the so-called "youthful" effects; will take care in selecting hats, furs, designs, trimmings, and accessories that are small enough not to make them seem topheavy; will select rather light colors; and will avoid all lines going from left to right which would tend to make them seem shorter and stouter. 

But never forget that to be little and birdlike is essentially feminine-so do not deny the rest of us the pleasure of your littleness all the time. The small woman who can achieve a tailored dignity in her daytime clothing, then metamorphose into a fluffy wee bundle of daintiness with her afternoon and evening clothes, is a delight. 

Some Special Line Hints for All You know what a line is, where to find it, how to use it, what a horizontal line does, and what a vertical line does, and of course you know what they both are. This hint for the well-proportioned figures that do not need any corrective lines: they must not affect too many lines of only one type in the ensemble.

Here are some special suggestions that may not have been covered elsewhere in this chapter: 195

Lovely Ladies Broad, Square Shoulders 

IN THE first place, you can relax your shoulders, and then have all your neck lines cut out toward the shoulder instead of near the neck. Avoid a long, unbroken sweep of material from the base of your neck to your sleeve seam, and have all of your sleeves set high, never low on the shoulders, which just broadens them the more. Break the space between your sleeve seam and your neck line with trimming in the form of tucks, braid, embroidery, or print in the material. 

Very Narrow, Sloping

Shoulders TO MINIMIZE narrow, sloping shoulders, you can affect the bâteau or boat neck, which likewise must be considerably lower in the front, and then straight across. And you can of course considerably broaden your shoulders by having your sleeve seam come lower on them, although this does not take away the sloping effect. The best thing to do for too narrow, sloping shoulders is to wear broad lace collars and fichus of the bertha variety and tiny capes whenever it is practical. 

Sleeves and Bracelets 

BRACELETS naturally cut your length of arm and shorten it, therefore avoid them if your arms are too short for grace. And try to select a becoming sleeve and affect it in all of your garments. If you have large arms never wear tight sleeves, or large, puffed sleeves. If you have arms that are thin to the point of "skinniness" don't expose them to the 196

You and Your Lines vulgar gaze of the multitude when they might look really pretty under lace or chiffon. 

And then, your hands! The one saving grace of the ugly leg o' mutton sleeves and bustles and street-cleaning trains was the soft, graceful, mitten-like lace-and-chiffon cuffs worn long over the knuckles and tied at the wrists with black velvet ribbons. Such a pretty style, such a graceful way to soften either too thick, or too plump, or too large hands! 

You Have a 

Back to Your

Neck THE back of the neck is usually the one place that you forget to keep well groomed! You keep it clean, and usually well shingled, if you have short hair, but it never seems to occur to even the most lovely ladies that it requires more attention than that. 

Give yourself a ruthless five minutes with a couple o mirrors, and study the back of your neck. Is it pretty?

Or is it long and prickly looking? Does it curve unbecomingly? Don't leave all this to everyone to see when you can just as easily select hats, collars, and scarfs that will make it attractive, or, at least, will not expose it so mercilessly to the public gaze. 

Look the back of your head and neck squarely in the face, just once!

WE ALL have hips, more or less, usually more. If they are the only out-of-proportion part of your anatomy be ruthless with them. Not only exercise, but use the proper 197

Lovely Ladies girdles and even the little short hip corsets that are so beneficial, especially if your work keeps you sitting all day, which would cause any hips to soften and spread. Don't neglect to corset them on the grounds that it is oldfashioned. Those

Feminin- Ninny Hips 

Don't define them to the entire world with sashes, decorative girdles, belts, or hip bands and covering of any kind. The proper line to make the hips as small as possible is the rather form-fitting one across the back of them, and no belt whatsoever. Never, for instance, have any flares or fulness on the sides of your skirt, and if you must have a belt make it stop short within two or three inches of your sides, at the back. Sometimes Limbs

LIMBS are not always all that they should be. You can do many things to assist them to a becoming slenderness, as I have already told you in the chapter on Beauty. But what can one do for knees that are knobby? Nothing, I am afraid, except not to expose them to jibes. 

If your legs, for any reason, are not all that they should be to be consistent with symmetry and beauty, correct the fault if you can, but above all wear the lines that make this the least conspicuous. Flared and pleated skirts, for instance, are much more flattering to heavy legs than are tight skirts. Short skirts never! Then, one can affect long skirts in the evening, or the robe de style type of dress. And always one can wear skirts with irregular hem 198

You and Your Lines lines, because they are always graceful, regardless of the mode.

Long or Short Waist Lines OCCASIONALLY Nature has a merry little way of distributing our growth so that it gets a little too rambunctious in places. 

Sometimes she will give us the type of figure that leaves us wishing she had made our limbs a bit longer or not so long. A long-waisted figure is never very graceful, nor is a very short-waisted figure.

In these days it is not necessary to show either of these in our clothing. Only you must realize your particular fault and select your waist and girdle accordingly, the waist line higher, in the case of the long-backed figure that is too short of limb; lower in the case of the shortwaisted figure that is too long of limb. What might nicely rest upon the hip bones of one woman would be all wrong resting there on another.

And both of these types should pay attention to the morning exercises that develop graceful carriages and easy posture. Earrings 

JUST one thing more earrings. Can you wear them or can you not? And if so, do you know when it is good taste to wear them or do you not?

Earrings are a definite line near your face. The woman with a round, plump face must at all costs avoid little button earrings that perch on the lobes of her ears, and broaden her face. Large women, very large women, should avoid little earrings, especially dangling ones. 199

Lovely Ladies 

Round earrings broaden your face naturally. The long thin face and the long thin neck are benefited by them, whereas the round face and short neck are benefited by long, drooping earrings. 200

Chapter X THE LOVELY LADY'S BEAUTY RITES 

"O Beauty, thou Goddess immortal, There is none other like to thee, none! 

Though we grave many names on thy portal 

The gold has not fallen from one."

THE present century may well be called the century of beautiful women. Although the early Greeks worshiped beauty as a religion, it is nevertheless a fact that there are more beautiful women to-day than ever before in the history of the world. 

Why? Simply because intelligence has shown us, and science has proved to us, that beauty, in terms of gracefulness and harmonious figural proportions, is achieved more often by intelligence than by chance. 

It is truth and not a platitude that every woman is born with the requisites of beauty. But she must consciously cultivate it. Rambling roses have a charm of their own but can never be compared to an exquisite American beauty rose, the product of cultivation. And does this development of your latent beauty, this care and protection, mean that you must spend hours and hours, days and days that you might spend much more pleasantly, attending upon the Goddess of Beauty? It does not! But 201

Lovely Ladies it does mean that the time you give her must be intelligently spent, that is all. Ten minutes in the morning and ten minutes at night is surely not too much of an investment.

And this is actually all that it does take, if you know just how to employ those minutes. 

You must simply learn a few not at all mysterious rites, every one of them based upon intelligence and understanding of the body, and perform themsecrets that only half a century ago were zealously guarded by the families to which they belonged, passed down from mother to daughter, and never disclosed to anyone else, not even one's best and dearest friend. This may sound as though they were complicated and must involve a lot of time, as indeed they may the first time or two that you practise them. But after a few days they will become as automatic as eating.

Making Beauty Rites a 

Habit 

FIRST, every woman must know certain things about her body, about skin, eyes, hair, and physique in general. Next, the way to care for them and the time. Certain ministrations in the morning, others at night, still others twice a week, especially after twenty, and yet 202

others under certain circumstances and weather conditions we encounter throughout our lives.

Then there are corrective beauty measures with which every woman can obviate blemishes. And last, cosmetic art-the intelligent and proper use of cosmetics, sometimes called make-up. Let's discuss all of these things in this chapter in their approximate order. 

THE foundation of all beauty is health. 

Beauty's 

A little attention to diet, to elimination, Foundation to correct breathing and posture will assure you of the lovely complexion, bright eyes, and glossy hair that are indispensable to beauty. And a bit more attention to daintiness, so easily acquired and kept, will further assure you of womanly beauty and charm. 

You cannot realize the true value of your womanhood unless you are healthy. This, of course, does not apply to the exceptional woman who is an invalid yet has developed her spiritual self to such a degree that one is never cognizant of her frailness. But the chances are that you are not this exceptional woman, and that ill health reacts unfavorably on you and on everyone around you. Therefore take immediate steps to correct it-drag out your little neurosis, look it right in the face, and realize that to every word of sympathy or affection inspired by pity you get when you are ailing, you have ten of praise and admiration when you are healthy. 

Here are the beauty rites of a lovely lady! Study them, learn them, practise them. Let's just take them one at a 203

Lovely Ladies time and then later put them in a sort of morning, night, daily, weekly, seasonal program. 

The Skin 

EVERYONE knows at last that the primary necessity for having and keeping a lovely skin is cleanliness. And the skin not only of the face but the body as well. The same general suggestions apply for one as the other but we will consider care of the face now. 

A little experimenting is all that you will need to ascertain which method of cleansing is the best for your type of skin. Whether you find it to be cleansing with oils or cleansing with water is largely dependent upon the texture of your skin, the quality of water you have in your locality, and climatic conditions.

Be that as it may, you must cleanse your face thoroughly once a day, and give it another cleansing, which need not be quite so thorough, as well. The thorough cleansing is best indulged in before retiring, save that there is the danger of one's being too tired and therefore being sorely tempted to let it go "just this once"-a cardinal crime which no really lovely lady would ever commit!

And now, before I go any farther and tell you how to cleanse by both methods, let me give you one beauty secret which has been in my family for generations. It seems upon first thought to be a silly thing, and one cannot see where it could be of any particular importance. But it is not a silly thing at all, and it is of importance! 

Water varies in different cities and different countries and very often is harsh and even injurious to the skin. 204

And sadly enough, you usually cannot tell this until the damage has been done. Remember grandmother's widemouthed cookie jar and the other one that was always full of wee, gorgeously striped red-and-white peppermints? 

Well, my grandmamma had another wide-mouthed jar on her dressing table that was always full of tiny gauze sacs, tied at the mouth with white thread. When she went a-voyaging a fresh supply of them nestled in a silver filigree box-which I myself have to this day, full of the same tiny, mysterious white bundles, the secret of which I am somewhat reluctant even now to reveal to you. 

Here is the secret: The little gauze bags contain nothing more precious nor mysterious than ordinary rolled oats (uncooked, of course), almond meal, and powdered orris root, in the proportion of one half oatmeal and one quarter each of the other two, all mixed together dry before being wrapped in tiny squares of gauze or cheesecloth.

And their exact mission in life is to be dropped into the wash basin, or the larger ones into the bathtub, and squeezed until they make the water quite milky. Especially if you cleanse your face always with water are these indispensable. 

Cleopatra, Sheba, Potiphar's wife, and many other beautiful women of history are said to have bathed in milk. Had 

Potiphar's wife but known the secret of the little gauze sack she need not have had the twenty she-asses accompany her on her jaunts in Potiphar's wake when he went to battle. 

Which logically leads me to tell you just how to wash 205

Lovely Ladies your face with soap and water, and later I will tell you about the cleansing with cream. Remember: you must ascertain for yourself just which type of cleansing is the best for your skin.

Washing Your Face 

ALL advice to the contrary, I should never use very hot water on my face, nor steam, unless for some special corrective purpose of which I will tell you later. Some faces can stand hot water and steam and apparently never be hurt by them, but most faces cannot. Therefore, fill your basin with warm water, drop in the little gauze bag, which is about an inch in diameter, and squeeze it until the water is milky, then dip in your soft face cloth and rub it with a good face soap. Your face soap can afford to be good, and rather expensive, since the ingredients used in a good soap are rather expensive in themselves. And since a cake of face soap that you use only for your face lasts a couple of months at least, it is not extravagance. 

Now rub your face gently with the face cloth and soap, never pulling it, never scrubbing it violently, and always with the cloth quite wet; wash your neck also, in an upward movement. Then wash the soap out of your face cloth under another faucet, the bathtub faucet, if it is handy. Last, dip your face cloth again into the water in the basin, and rinse your face well, once, twice, and three times, until every hidden speck of soap is out of it. 

Now a quick dash of cold water, also taken from the bathtub tap, and your face is ready to dry on a soft towel. 206

By the way, more harm is done your face with a rough towel or vigorous scrubbing than by the elements. Try to consider your face at all times as a flower.

In the same milky water in the basin then wash your hands and arms, and rinse them also in cold water. Never neglect this final cold-water rinse, which tones the skin and keeps it firm.

Oil Cleansing 

CLEANSING the face with oil or cream is a different matter. If the water does not quite agree with your skin, yet you do not feel clean unless you do wash with it, then just use the water twice a week and the cream the rest of the time. 

After motoring or hiking or any unusual exposure to dirt or soot use both cleansings—you will find that a goodly amount of dirt detaches itself after each one. Use whichever one first that you wish, preferably the water.

For cream or oil cleansing take a generous dab of whatever cream you are using in the hollow of your hand and spread it on your face; massage this well into the skin with the circular upward movement with which we have all become familiar. Do this with the tips of the fingers and not too hard. One is inevitably tempted to pull and push the face into shape, hoping to correct in a week what would naturally take years. This injures and does no good.

After at least five minutes of this massaging, which includes the neck and base of the neck, wipe all of the grease and dirt off on a very soft towel or silky paper. I know a 207

Lovely Ladies famous actress who uses for face towels beautifully hemmed squares of cream-coloured velvet! Don't laugh.

After the initial expense they are no more costly than towels of other materials, and launder back to their original softness, if not their original beauty.

There are innumerable lotions on the market which you can use, in liquid form, of course, after a cream cleansing to take every bit of oily feeling away and leave the skin refreshed as does the cold-water dash. If convenient, rub your face quickly with a piece of ice after this cream cleansing, only hold it in the towel so that your hands will not be chilled.

After the cream cleansing of your face, massage your hands in a good washing movement, with the cream, before you wash them with soap and water. Massaging the hands just this little bit each day will help to keep them plump and youthful.

Let's leave the skin for a while, and take another important asset to beauty, the hair.

A Romance of 

Lovely Hair

LOVELY Godiva, fair as a dappled sky at dawn and as sweet as the whistle of the blackbird in the spring. And the greatest glory of her fresh Saxon beauty was the wondrous flaxen hair that fell in two heavy braids of dull gold to the ground. And one day, for the sake of the poor suffering townspeople who worshiped her from afar, she rode through the streets of the town on a milk-white mare with no covering save the golden glory of her hair. 208

For the black-browed Earl, her husband, a hard man and cruel, had sworn an oath that only thus could she win mercy for his subjects. It pleased him to jest, for he did not think that she could, in her purity, turn what he accounted evil to good.

The streets through which she passed were empty and silent. It was as if some fearsome plague had swept the town and left no living man within. The cobblestoned alleys, the corner by the tavern, the little crooked courts-all were empty and heavy with silence. The click of the white mare's shoes on the cobblestones rang through the streets like an angelus. And every window was barred and locked and shuttered. This had the people done for her, in reverence to their lady of mercy who dwelt in the grim castle on the hill. 

Anyone can have lovely hair! 

Lovely hair is hair of any color, length, thickness, that 208 is healthy and clean. All that you need to assure yourself of is this health, which will come mainly from cleanliness, and you will have lovely hair. Beautiful hair is glossy hair.

Glossy hair is well-kept hair. And well-kept hair is healthy 209

Lovely Ladies hair, neither too oily nor too dry, since either condition is abnormal and undesirable. 

Before we go any farther here let me tell you one little secret of lovely hair, and that is brilliantine. A wee bitnot enough to make your hair look greasy, will add immensely to its beauty. Brunettes can use brilliantine more frequently than can blondes. Brushing 

Waves and

Curls 

You have heard since you were a child that you should brush your hair frequently—a hundred strokes, I believe, is the proverbial nightly ministration. But to brush your hair with a dirty brush and to thus brush the day's dirt into it does more harm than not brushing it at all. And no woman wants to pay for a marcel or curl only to brush it out again at night! The exact reasons for frequent hair-brushing are cleanliness and scalp stimulation. But brushing is not absolutely necessary, for there are other ways to keep the scalp and hair clean, and you can stimulate the scalp with your finger tips without disturbing your curl at all. 

Oily Hair

HAIR that is too oily is always unattractive and difficult to manage. Not only that, but the overstimulation of oil glands will result in faded, lifeless hair and even premature grayness and baldness.

Oily hair is sometimes the result of too frequent sham- 210

pooing. One tries to correct this condition by washing the scalp, which is a great mistake, as it only stimulates the oil glands to further effort. If, instead, you will not wash your hair for at least six weeks, giving it a dry shampoo twice a week and a correct and persistent scalp massage two or three times a day, your hair will regain its natural health and glossiness and keep it as long as you give it the proper care.

Fluffing and drying the hair in the sun, but not while it is wet, is another aid in the correction of an oily scalp; let it have air and sunshine whenever possible. Even select your hats with the idea of giving it as much ventilation as you can. Simple, isn't it? But possibly this very simplicity causes you to overlook the means at hand to correct that ugly oiliness and keep your hair lovely. 

A DRY scalp is invariably a tight scalp.

Dry Scalp 

And the only thing required to correct this condition is the loosening of the scalp with massage. 

Could anything be simpler?

Of course everyone knows that the proper way to massage the hair is not to rub it with the fingers in a way that might destroy the new hairs, but to plant the fingers very firmly on the scalp and then rotate the scalp itself rather than rub it. If your scalp is dry and you do this twice a day for a week you will notice an improvement. Too frequent shampoos are as injurious to a dry scalp as to an oily one. 

And one must not forget that the hair can be kept as clean and possibly cleaner with proper brushing. 211

Lovely Ladies THERE are dry shampoos of various Dry Shampoos descriptions on the market, but they are all mainly compounded of powdered orris root. Therefore, the plain powdered orris root will do just as well. Corn meal is another excellent dry shampoo for the scalp, particularly for very dry hair. If your hair is extremely fine 

I suggest half and half.

Simply rub this well into your hair and scalp, wrap your head in a soft towel for an hour or so, and then brush out the shampoo and dirt with a clean brush. Two strokes of your clean brush will convince you that you are actually cleaning your hair! Have a clean bath towel at hand and clean the brush on the bath towel after each stroke. 

They Call It Dandruff 

SO-CALLED dandruff is not to be feared. It is a perfectly natural shedding of the old skin of the scalp, and more recently has come to be called cuticle. But it certainly is unsightly when it is excessive. Nothing will correct this more quickly than a morning and evening massage, and it is worth spending ten minutes twice a day just to correct this unloveliness, because of course after a week or ten days you will only need to spend a few minutes a day to keep your hair in condition.

At the same time give yourself about three hot oil massages a week. Do not put too much oil on your hairindeed, not so much that it will even show. Just heat a couple of tablespoonfuls of olive oil, pour it in a saucer, 212

seat yourself at a table with a good book propped in front of you, and massage away!

After any abnormal hair condition is corrected, such as dandruff, too oily or too dry hair, brittle, broken, or lifeless hair, a very little intelligent daily care will keep your hair lovely. In this day and age there is hardly such a thing as natural beauty of any kind. All beauty bespeaks determined effort on the part of its possessor. 

And Then the 

Hair You 

Don't Want 

UNWANTED hair is a trial to almost every woman, I believe. Whether it be those unsightly hairs on the legs that make grotesque hieroglyphics under chiffon hose, or hair on the forearms or face, we all have some hair problem.

A depilatory or a razor is the simplest remedy. Don't worry about the hairs growing in heavier and more stubborn, for you will never let the hairs grow long enough to have to worry about them. Really unsightly hairs can be removed by electrolysis, but the fine, aggravating growth cannot. Use the depilatory or razor everywhere you wish to remove hair, and use either one persistently and faithfully. In time, especially with the depilatory, the hair may give up the effort and refuse to grow back in. 

If you will faithfully wash the unwanted hair with peroxide one day and diluted ammonia the next, then peroxide the following, and so on, in time all but the most stubborn growth will rot at the roots. But I do not advise this for either your face or underarms. 213

Lovely Ladies 

Another very effective method, and one that I like because it does leave the skin so nice and smooth, is to rub the area with a cream after you have removed the hairs by shaving or using a depilatory, and then rub the surface with a flat pumice stone-but not so hard that you take the skin off. Then rub for a few moments morning and evening with the pumice stone moistened in toilet water. 

The hair probably will not return, but if it does repeat the process a few times. The hair will certainly sooner or later give up in despair. 

Now you must learn how to care for your eyes.

The Eyes of Beauty 

"O inconnue, je suis enamoré de tes yeux!"

Thus mourned the Chimera as he hovered over the face of the Sphinx.

The eyes of beauty are the eyes of intelligence. No woman can be unattractive if she has lovely eyes, and lovely eyes are not a matter of size, color, or shape. They need only be sparkling, interesting, and alive.

We are not considering eye troubles and defects here, which should have the immediate attention of a specialist, but the average, everyday care that will keep the eyes strong and beautiful. The first habit to establish is that of cleanliness, something too often overlooked in regard to the eyes. Morning and night, after every harsh exposure, and as often during the day as convenient, a few drops of a good eye wash will do wonders-with a medicine dropper, preferably instead of an eye cup.

In the Hollywood cinema studios, where eyes are cer- 214

tainly taxed to their uttermost endurance, they use collyrium, and many opticians now claim that it is better for the eyes than boric acid. 

Motoring, or any anticipated eye strain, should be provided for with colored glasses. Not, indeed, those awful things that you buy in any drug store, of green, amber, or smoked glass - they are more harmful than helpfulbut faintly darkened glasses that have been prescribed for you by your own optician. The expense will pay for itself many times, since one does not change these glasses very often. 

Have you any idea what a tax it is to the eyes to spend a day at the beach, with the glare of the sun on the water and sand? Consider what one summer does to your beach parasol -and remember that it is sturdy canvas and paint. Also, the salt water encountered while bathing is often too harsh for the eyes and should be washed out with eye wash later, to prevent any inflammation. 

Regardless of your vanity, any eye weakness should be overcome with glasses, worn at least in the house if not on the street. It is not only the possible consequences to your 215

Lovely Ladies eyes that you should consider, but the strain to your nerves and health generally-and wrinkles! 

YOUR eyelashes and eyebrows can and 

Eyelashes Are will respond to treatment, if you but per- 

Important sist in it. Simply brush your eyebrows with a not too soft little brush dipped in cold water every night and every morning, in the curve that you wish them to grow. After doing this at night dip the brush into yellow vaseline, olive oil, or one of the good lash and brow growers that you can secure on the market, and brush your eyebrows in the same direction. 

Now your eyelashes. Brush them up and out (with your eyes open, never down with your eyes closed) with the same brush dipped in the eyelash grower.

It may take a year before you see any results, but what of it? By that time this care will have become as automatic a gesture as powdering your nose.

We come now to hands, the servants of the soul. 

The Poetry of 

Beautiful Hands

ISEULT OF THE WHITE HANDS, they called her. Famous were the hands of Iseult the beautiful, so that men made stories about them, and wove strange fancies of their magic. Full of healing they were, and full of mystery, fluttering restlessly like white birds, delicate as carved ivory, fragile as ancient china. 

Tristan came to her out of the West. A brave knight he was, who knew the heat of battle and the lust of victory. 216

But he came to her weary of life. And with the white hands of Iseult over his eyes he forgot the lust of battle, the warriors' curse, the drinking songs, and the scars that armor cannot guard against. Her small hands, like white birds flying, sought his stern face, and their coolness soothed the pain of his wandering.

But he was a knight, and the neighing of battle steeds was in his blood. He went away again. And Iseult stayed, for woman cannot follow the warrior.

Long she waited, long she sat watching the west, though she knew he would not come again. 

And the white hands, forsaken, lay stricken in her lap; lay like broken flowers, weary of weaving the endless hours into a poem of sighs.

One can no more picture a beautiful woman without hands than a flower without a stem. It is our hands that express our animation, our graceful aliveness. And no matter what their contour or texture, with the proper 217

Lovely Ladies care they can be lovely. And this proper care consists merely of protecting them from the elements at all times and massaging them with skin food, not only to supplement the natural oils which you wash out of them, but to keep them plump and therefore unwrinkled and young.

Your hand lotion should be almost a religion. A dainty woman will have a jar of hand lotion not only in the bathroom but in every other spot in the house where one might have occasion to wash the hands, and on her dressing table as well. Especially will she take a jar to her office with her if she is a business woman. Never wash your hands with warm water and soap that you do not give them afterward a dash of cold water and then a bit of the hand lotion. This lotion can be any one of the excellent preparations on the market, or a mild solution of glycerine and lemon juice, or the good old glycerine and rose water.

About Your 

Nails

A LOVELY lady does not rush to the manicurist once a week and expect her to repair seven days' neglect in half an hour. Her daily care of her nails makes a drastic manicure unnecessary for they are really manicured all the time. The use of a nailbrush with hot water and soap and the nightly application of a bit of vaseline or hot olive oil to the cuticle will keep them in the proper condition. One uses an orange stick to cleanse them, never scraping the inside with a file or allowing anyone else to do so. Naturally, if the inside surface is rough discoloration will be much 218

harder to remove than if the surface is smooth. A wee box of powdered pumice into which you dip your orange stick will remove the more stubborn stains.

Liquid polish is especially attractive for evening but rather hard on the nails and difficult to keep even and smooth. And of course unless it should happen to become a fad, too highly polished nails are not good taste. 

The preceding information is only for the average maintenance, but any special discoloration of the hands or nails naturally requires special treatment. Work that is constantly discoloring to the hands is no excuse for unkemptness. You simply have to give them a little more attention than otherwise, that is all.

Whitening 

Hands Quickly 

TO GAIN quick results for a very bad stain 

I suggest none other than the good old clay pack that became such a fad a few seasons ago, and which is very little more and certainly no less than fuller's earth. Make up a paste of this with water and cover your hands with it, then let it stay on for at least a half hour after it dries. Don't move your fingers the least bit while this is on them because it will crack the clay and possibly the skin with it. Have a basin of warm water ready before you put the clay on and plunge your hands into this when you wish to remove it.

Don't use clay often on your hands, because it dries and wrinkles them. It is only for the occasional emergency, when your hands are badly stained and you need to whiten them in a hurry. 219

Lovely Ladies 

At night you may put on rubber gloves filled with a not too wet bran or corn-meal mush. Have someone tie the gloves to your wrists, not tightly enough to stop circulation, and endure it for one night. The very worst stain cannot hold out against a few nights of this. 

And you need not wear rubber gloves only when correcting bad stains. You can very easily acquire the habit of wearing them every night, just to keep the skin food on your hands and away from the bed clothes. Then, too, they cause the hands to perspire a bit and thus draw out all loitering particles of grime. If the rubber gloves are too cold and clammy to suit you a pair of fabric gloves will do almost as well, or any of the cosmetic gloves already on the market. And don't, I beg of you, become discouraged because you don't sleep so well the first night, because you can and should get used to wearing the gloves. 

Each night before retiring, and after you have attended to your face, eyes, and hair, wash your hands with warm water and soap. Then use a dash of cold water, massage them deeply and smoothly a few minutes with a good skin food, which you at the same time rub well into the cuticle, and then put on your cosmetic gloves.

Simple lanolin to which you have added a few drops of tincture of benzoin is about the most effective skin food for the hands you could possibly find. But you must remember that it is natural for the plumpness to fade away from the backs of hands the while the veins and tendons enlarge, so do not think that the lanolin will be or continue to be effective unless you use it persistently. 220

If you know that you have lovely hands you will use them gracefully, and they will enhance your charm and express your personality as nothing else can or will do. 

Routine and Habit

Now that you know all of the little secrets that a lovely lady should know, suppose we make out a daily program, a different one for morning and night. After that, I can give you some special hints for things you should do once or twice a week, and some other special summer and winter suggestions. 

You must systematize these things, else you are all at sixes and sevens with yourself and your time, and you naturally lose interest in even such a vital matter if it requires too much time and concentration. But it doesn't. Surely ten minutes in the morning and ten at night is not too much to offer to Beauty!

Morning 

ESTABLISH the habit of taking a glass of hot water with lemon upon arising. You can have the lemon juice all ready the night before. It is a delightful "eye opener" of a chilly morning, and does wonders to start your "interior decorators" functioning for the day.

Next, your few moments of deep breathing by the open window. Only a few, but what a difference they make in your circulation, in the sparkle of your eyes and glow of your skin! 

Then a few exercises. You should know which ones you particularly need, and if not, a thorough bending and 221

Lovely Ladies stretching will be enough. Remember, five slow exercises are better than fifty rapid ones. It is just for a muscle stretching, to insure suppleness and grace. What is the immediate index to old age? A rigid, strained carriage. 

Five minutes a morning with the proper exercises will ward this off indefinitely. 

Do you know what exercising actually does to your muscles? It simply squeezes the blood out of them as you contract them and permits, or demands, rather, an influx of fresh blood as they relax. This is what keeps them firm and supple. 

Now a quick tub, finishing with a cold shower if you can stand it. And you really should accustom yourself to this if possible. 

Then a quick hair brushing to make the hair lustrous, the application of your complexion aids and make-up, which I tell you about later, the arranging of your coiffure, and last of all your nails and hand lotion. Now you are ready for whatever the day has to offer, fortified with the knowledge of your natural beauty, daintiness, and therefore charm.

At Night 

NEVER, for any reason, retire without giving yourself the very few ministrations that are so absolutely essential to loveliness. A mild bath will both relax and refresh you and insure a perfect repose. 

And what dainty woman will not have refreshing bath salts for her bath, even though they be a luxury! This is one of the little things that makes a lovely lady. And, by 222

the way, if you feel that bath salts are a bit of a luxury, drop a tablespoonful of powdered orris root into your bath, and see how nice it is. You can make a very inexpensive and refreshing bath salt by simply buying refined or powdered Epsom salts, which you ask for in just that way, and fine table salt. Keep a wide-mouthed jar with a tight stopper on hand, fill with half and half of the Epsom and table salts, add about two teaspoons of your favorite perfume, and cork tightly. Do not open the bottle at all for about a week. 

After your bath comes your thorough face cleansing, of which I have told before. Next a freshening lotion or a dash of cold water to remove the greasy feeling, and then a good skin food and tissue builder, which you massage into your face with the tips of your fingers gently but thoroughly. Remember that you need not use a heavy coating of this skin food, since it is your face that needs it and not the pillow slip. In fact, you should wipe off the surplus with your soft towel.

I could not and would not if I could recommend any particular creams or cosmetics. This you simply must do for yourself, but you must understand that it is neither fair to your skin nor to the cosmetic you are trying to use it only a few nights and then discard it because you do not see instant, miraculous results. 

And then your eye wash. Your eyes must be clean as well as your face, particularly after the wear and tear and strain that they are subjected to during the day. I have told you just how and why to use this eye wash. Then a 223

Lovely Ladies tiny, firm brush rubbed over your eyebrows a few times to stimulate and shape them. It may be dipped in a little vaseline or olive oil, or in any one of the eyebrow and lash growers on the market. I knew a lovely lady who used one of those advertised lash growers for eight months, not expectantly but resignedly, when, lo, her eyelashes simply grew out to twice their length all at once! 

Speaking of eyelashes, shall I ever forget the time my kitchen gas stove exploded and left me quite sadly denuded of eyebrows and fringed eyelashes for two months, only to have them grow out again nearly twice as long and as thick as they were before. But I really am not advising you to try this! 

You can, however, singe your eyelashes in quite as effective and a much safer manner. Simply take a heavy wire hairpin, bend it out straight, embed one end of it firmly in a cork, and then hold the other end in a flame. 

Wipe any paint off of this carefully with paper before you attempt to singe your eyelashes with it. And don't do this at all unless you are quite sure that your hands are steady enough to attempt it, because you can get a very nasty burn from it and even injure your eyeball badly. However, if you attempt it do it yourself and do not let anyone else try to do it for you. 

Almost last is your hair, which you brush and comb out, not forgetting at the same time to give your scalp a few minutes' massage. Finally, there are your hands, which you wash, dry, massage with skin food, and slip into rubber or cosmetic gloves. 224

And now you are ready for a beneficial and restorative repose. 

By the way, do you know why Oriental women have such beautiful throats and necks, which remain unwrinkled all of their lives? Because they sleep without a pillow! And you have no idea how truly easy it is to acquire the habit of doing this. However, it is of no benefit at all to sleep without a pillow on your side-it must be flat on your back. 

Lie, then, flat on your back, your hands and arms at either side encased in cosmetic gloves, and consign yourself to Beauty! She will be most wondrous kind to you through the quietly marching hours of the night.

And now, just to help you to establish this morning and evening routine, here is your program made out in order. 

Copy it and pin it up on the wall over your dressing table. 

In a week the schedule will have become a habit, but a habit that you will enjoy all your life. This does not include your weekly or semi-weekly facial and other corrective beauty aids. It is the normal, everyday routine.
