I HAVE given you the daily routine in which every lovely lady indulges throughout her life. But after twenty there are other things that you must do once, twice, and later three times a week, to frustrate the gentle fingers of time. First 

I want to mention corrective beauty measures.

Any abnormal condition in your body that results in a loss of beauty and discomfort and even embarrassment requires the attention of a doctor, preferably a specialist, but under no circumstances a beauty doctor or so-called 226

"quack." For example, for any type of skin eruptions consult a dermatologist.

Here is a simple remedy that I have tried successfully on a friend of mine who had had such an eruption for over six months, and had tried various things and been to several dermatologists.

A Simple Remedy for 

Clearing 

Complexions the night. 

EVERY night she put fuller's earth on her face, just mixed with water, and allowed it to dry for forty-five minutes. Then before going to bed, and after the clay was washed off she put zinc ointment on for the rest of 

She did this for a week, the first three nights putting the ointment on very heavily and covering it with cotton held in place by a bandage, both to keep the ointment on all night and to keep it from contact with the bed linen. 

After the first few days the cotton was not used. Every morning during this week the ointment was washed off with warm water and a very good face soap, which was massaged into the skin and rinsed off thoroughly. 

After a week of this, following the clay one night we washed her face with the warm water and face soap, and then pressed out all of the blackheads and whiteheads that would yield. Followed the zinc ointment again for the night. The next morning, after washing her face with warm water and soap again, she patted in a good, strong, fiery astringent. That night the clay again, and the ointment, the next morning the washing and the astringent. 227

Lovely Ladies 

That night the clay and the remainder of the stubborn blackheads were pressed out and the ointment applied.

Then we cut the program down to every other day, and it went something like this: 

Every morning, thorough face-washing with warm water and the very good face soap, followed by the sharp astringent applied every other morning. Every night, the zinc ointment. Every other night, the clay, followed by the zinc ointment.

Then it was cut down to twice a week and finally to once a week, but the daily face-washing was continued from then on. Of course, we regulated the diet, too, drastically cutting out all of the starches and sugars and oils for one month, and then allowing them only in very small amounts.

After the skin was quite healed there of course remained blotches and ugly scars that did not disappear for several months. But they went as the skin peeled and became new again.

Should you become stout suddenly-not gradually over a period of years, from which you would realize that you were eating too much and too many of the wrong things, but suddenly-consult a gland specialist. If you become suddenly thin, without any effort on your part, consult a physician at once, as this may portend something serious. 

If any part of your body does not conform to your standards of beauty, and you know that this is not a family characteristic, consult a physician. You may find that it is 228

a little thing that will respond at once to treatment, therefore it should not be endured. 

Careful of Beauty Operations IN THE hands of skilled surgeons, the most astonishing operations for beauty are possible nowadays. But never trust yourself to beauty specialists and operators for anything as serious as a corrective operation. They do perform them, and successfully. But every now and then something goes wrong. 

If the fault is a family characteristic you can correct it yourself and should begin to do so determinedly when you are very young. Hereditary double chins, round, sloping, too narrow, or straight, stiff shoulders, bow-legs or their antithesis, protruding abdomen, defective bust development, ankles that turn-each of these can be corrected.

It is a good thought to keep that family beauty is a matter of breeding, of many generations of beautiful people who have taken care of their beauty, had a proper conception and appreciation of beauty, and eliminated defects not only in themselves but rigidly in their children. 

If you do not therefore come naturally by beauty, be beauty's pioneer for your children. There is no more worth-while ambition. It can be done, and this is the most praiseworthy and important mission of womanhood -to give beauty to the world as expressed primarily in her own body.

But the chances are that you have no very serious im- 229

Lovely Ladies perfections that just the average daily care and the required once or twice a week routine will be quite enough. 

Now I must tell you of what the weekly routine consists. 

Face Care After Twenty 

ABOUT twice a week, and oftener after you are thirty, you will need a facial, which you can give yourself at home. If you have been neglecting your skin for a long time you will need one every day-to get the muscles and the underskin of your face in proper condition. Start to-day, not to-morrow or next week or "pretty soon." Devote to this ministration time in which you know that you will not be interrupted.

And always lie flat on your back and relax completely while the facial is drying on your face. To take a facial while you are in an upright position is far more harmful than beneficial, as it dries and tautens the muscles in a sagging position.

Correct Facial 

Procedure 

THERE is one correct way to take a facial. 

I am going to give you the method, and later suggest certain facials for you to use.

The same method of application does for all. The first thing is to cleanse your face thoroughly, using either the soap-and-water method, of which I have already told you, or the cream of oil one. I think twice a week it would be a very good idea to cleanse the face with soap and water, and let this be just before the facial. Don't forget the little gauze bag! 

Next spread a bath towel where your head will repose 230

on the pillow. Then apply your facial while standing up, and lie down immediately on your back, close your eyes, relax, relax, relax. Conjure up beautiful things, pleasant things, lovely thoughts that will make you lovely. If you cannot think of anything else, recall to yourself the melody of one of your favorite songs, or a beautiful bouquet of flowers. And don't think this silly, because it isn't. 

Before you lie down prepare a basin with a little warm water in which has been squeezed one of the little gauze bags. This water is for washing the facial off after its half hour of drying, since you put the preparation on your eyelids as well, and sometimes find it awkward to fill the basin afterward. In washing your face be very careful not to pull the muscles in a downward direction.

Ice as a Tonic and Astringent 

IMMEDIATELY after your face is washed rub it with a piece of ice, a flat piece that you have wrapped in a piece of gauze, so that it will not injure your skin and your hands will be kept from chilling. Chilled hands may have serious consequences. If ice is not available, cold water will do, but do not merely dash it a couple of times on your face-apply it with your face cloth repeatedly until the skin is quite tingling.

Next you may need to follow this with an astringent lotion, but not, of course, if your face is very firm. Depending upon the severity of the lotion, do not leave it on for more than a few minutes, and then remove it at once with your water or cream. 231

Lovely Ladies 

Your facial is complete. You will now pat in some of your skin food and wipe it off with a soft towel before you proceed with your make-up.

The Milk maid's Secret

HERE come some of the family secrets again. The first comes through the milkman! For the cow furnishes us with three very essential beauty aids, and with these alone, were you ever where other beauty aids were not accessible, you could furnish your complexion with everything it needed. 

Milk is a truly marvelous bleaching lotion, and is as well mildly astringent. Cream is a natural skin softener and nourisher. Sour milk has even more drastic bleaching propensities, if it is not too acid for your skin. For this latter reason I prefer buttermilk.

If your skin is inclined to be sallow, rub it with a piece of cotton dipped in milk every night before you retire, and leave the milk on all night. In the winter, when your skin is likely to become dry and chapped, rub it with table cream before you put on your make-up. However, if your face is inclined to have too many fine hairs, it is well to remember that both table cream and lanolin are rather conducive to encouraging its growth. 

And now for the buttermilk facial, without which I am sure my skin could not exist for more than a week.

Simply soak strips of absorbent cotton in the buttermilk and put them onto your face until it is quite covered. Lie flat on your back and relax for half an hour or longer until 232

the cotton is quite dry. Then remove it and follow the directions I just gave for completing a facial. 

You use sour milk in exactly the same way. A milk facial is delightful for a tender skin, and has milder bleaching propensities, as well as being astringent. For all three of these facials use strips of cotton. Another Humble Handmaiden 

THEN, too, Biddy, the hen, pays her tithe to the immortal goddess. After your face is prepared for a facial separate the white and the yolk of an egg. Apply a coating of the white, relax and lie flat on your back until this dries. Then apply a second coat of the white and wait until it also dries. Now apply a thick coating of the yellow and let this dry as well. You will feel it pulling out every blemish and tightening the sagging muscles of the face! Fan your face while it is drying, of course. 

Mother Earth as Well 

CLAY facial packs were no new story to many of us when they became such a fad a few seasons ago. We can look to Nature to supply us in a natural, usable form everything that we need, and it is just the sophistication of this age that causes us to wish to improve upon her. There are certain regions that furnish us with medicinal clays which are extremely beneficial to our complexions, and every face clay, despite advertising to the contrary, is basically composed of one of these clays, with perhaps some other in- 233

Lovely Ladies gredients added, and certainly some perfume, just to make it the more appealing. 

You can buy this clay in any drugstore, in almost any part of the world, by simply asking for fuller's earth. You mix it with water, smear it on your face, lie flat on your back and relax again, and let it dry. Remove the mud by first moistening it with your face cloth dipped in water. 

Or better still, have the wash basin full of tepid water and dip your whole face into this, letting it stay a few minutes to soften the clay, but carefully keeping your eyes closed.

Two facials a week and special care after any unusual exposure, added to your daily care, are all the complexion aids that you will ever need. But the whole secret is that you must do all of them regularly—you cannot suddenly decide to repair years of neglect and expect to achieve any results in a couple of weeks.

By the way, a small electric hair dryer is a worth-while investment. Dry your face for the facials with this, but with cold air, not with hot.

Weekly Care of Hands 

DAILY and nightly care is almost, not quite, enough. After unusual exposure to weather or chemicals, and once a week as well, you should give them a special "facial.”

One night a week set aside for the rubber gloves filled with corn-meal mush, or bran paste, or corn meal and kerosene. In a short while you will be so used to this that it will become a habit. 234

The night after this be sure to give them a good massage with lanolin and a few drops of tincture of benzoin, and then put on your cosmetic or rubber gloves in order to supplement any drying effect that the more astringent corn meal or bran had upon them. 

And Each

Week for Hair 

NIGHTLY and morning brushing and any temporary corrective measures are average care of the hair. Once a week you must have a thorough cleansing. Remember, I recommend the dry shampoo for this. Once a month or every six weeks is sufficient for a wet shampoo. In between you of course brush each day's dirt out of your hair with a clean hair brush. 

After the dry shampoo and brushing give your scalp a good twenty minutes' massage, or more, with your fingers dipped in hot oil if your hair is a bit dry or fine.

This is all that you will need to keep your hair in beauti- 235

Lovely Ladies ful condition. Of course, after illness or any unusual exposure there are other things, of which you will hear later. 

So much for the weekly care of your beauty. Now come some special summer and winter hints. Unfortunately, our epidermis has lost the art of changing itself to meet weather changes, so we must aid it a bit if we would protect it from permanent harm. 

Summertime 

WE ARE going for our vacation-happily, and anticipating the average dose of sunburn and freckles, and not caring very much about it either. But each year we notice that our skin reacts to after-summer treatment less and less quickly, and we realize, sometimes too late, that a little prevention would have done what months and even years of repairing cannot do. 

So this time let's not have any complexion tragedies that may be irreparable. There are such things to do to ward them off!

First, you must realize that tan and freckles are Nature's effort to protect the skin from harm. Hot sun, S O S of the skin, rush of the Color Pigment Corps, and there you have it. The worst of it all is that the C. P. Corps finds it so pleasant to stay there that it adopts your skin for its permanent residence, and only a long and valiantly waged war will ever rout it. 

And remember, bleaching freckles away once does not mean that they will stay away. Freckle creams at night 236

will take care of the bleaching, but "protection" means vanishing cream or something similar all of the time, and a great deal of attention to hats, parasols—yes, even veils when necessary. It's the price you must pay for the very beautiful skin which freckles easily. 

You can readily see that the first summer complexion care is to cover the exposed parts of your body with as heavy a coating of cream and powder as it will absorb. Especially is this so when you are going to motor, or to the beach. Never start on a motor trip any time of the year, either day or night, without your little tube of vanishing cream in your purse and extra face powder. 

And stop at two- or three-hour intervals and put more cream on your face and backs of hands, as much as they will absorb. 

Some Hints for the Beach 

BEFORE you leave the house for a swim, again before you go into the water, and immediately upon getting out of the water, cream and powder-no matter what anyone says or how many people look. Wear a beach hat, carry parasols-in fact, neglect nothing that will 237

Lovely Ladies serve as added protection to your skin. And double your night beauty work, especially for the first few days. 

Hair that is exposed to the sun while wet will lose much of its natural luster. This is true when it is shampooed as well as after you come out of the surf, and that is the reason why you should dry it as much as you can on a towel before you go into the sunshine. if During vacation time, especially if it includes a daily dip in the surf or pool, your hair will suffer in spite of the things you will do to protect it. However, it will suffer much more, and with more permanently injurious effects, you do not do your best to protect it. Every night you must supplement the natural oil that the sun and surf have taken from it, by rubbing into the scalp a bit of olive oil, castor oil, or vaseline. And you must wash the hair every day after your swim to get the salt out of it. Dry it on a towel, and never use a hair dryer if you can possibly avoid it.

When summer has come to a close take a few good scalp treatments, and be faithful with a tonic, just to get your hair into condition again, when the regular daily care outlined above will suffice.

The same general warning applies to your face. After vacation time, take a facial every day for a couple of weeks, until every vestige of tan, sunburn, or any other kind of blemish is removed.

Your hands as well come in for their share of extra summer care, and then corrective care afterward. What if you do have to endure the bran mush or even the corn 238

meal and kerosene for two weeks? Beautiful hands are well worth it!

Although I have already told you about wearing sun glasses, I must remind you again that especially for the unaccustomed glare your eyes will encounter upon your vacation you must have them always at hand. Never take a boat trip, an auto trip, or visit the seaside without them. 

And then be especially gentle with your eyes during vacation time, washing them free of salt water and other irritating, unaccustomed things that they will encounter. Winter Suggestions 

THE wind and Jack Frost seem to be in a heartless conspiracy against our beauty in these months, but if you have been taking care of your face and hands properly every night they hold no terrors for you. The logical thing to offset skin irritation from cold is more ample protection in the form of vanishing cream or a powder base, while a more lavish use of skin food at night will protect it the more. 

Your hands must have special winter attention. Never be in too much of a hurry to dry them thoroughly after washing before going out, and then to rub them with hand lotion. At night give them ample skin food in your cosmetic gloves. Remember that it is easier, far easier, to avert beauty disasters than to remedy them. Every lovely lady knows this!

AND now we come to the really interesting part, the one that is the most gratifying and encouraging, because the 239

Lovely Ladies 

And Now

Make-Up results are immediately observable. It is that of make-up, without which no woman to-day can be her loveliest. The day is long past when the discreet use of cosmetics with which to enhance natural charms was considered bad form. Nowadays one cannot deny the gracious help of little round jars, artificial to be sure, but perfectly in keeping with the more sophisticated, artistic trend of the age. 

But first I want you to understand the real reason why "when

Nature fails to do her part, we help her slightly with our art." 

At that she doesn't really fail to do her part. It is rather that these unleisurely times make too insistent demands upon her. 

Our purpose is simply to capture her too fleeting gifts and keep them in tangible form after she has withdrawn them. Too, one can do wonderful corrective things to plain features with make-up! Remember that no woman in the world was ever born without a single requisite for beauty. And that no matter how nondescript your features might be, if you have a good complexion-which any woman on earth can have-glossy, healthy hair-which also any woman 24.0

can have and know just a few rules for the proper application of make-up, you can be beautiful. 

But please do one thing. Learn that just to know your proper make-up is not enough-a thing that not one woman in a hundred actually knows. You can no more use the same make-up all of the time than you can wear a party frock on the golf links or a sports suit to a ball. There is something actually repellent about a vivid make-up in the daytime. One cannot even wear the same type of make-up for out-of-door sports that you can wear for other activities during the day. The correct evening make-up is another matter again, one which depends largely upon what lighting arrangement you will encounter in the place to which you are going.

Let me now give you the few basic make-up suggestions for your type. You yourself gauge the proportions of each for the different occasions.

Preparing Your Face 

IT IS in the morning that you prepare your face to receive its make-up, as well as for the encounters of the day. I have already told you just how you remove all this and take care of your skin at night. 

First, you cleanse your face of its night cream and any natural bodily waste that it has acquired while you slumbered. If you are leaving the house immediately, do not do this cleansing with soap and water, but with cleansing cream.

The next step after the cleansing is a freshening lotion, 241

Lovely Ladies to take away the greasy feeling and prepare your skin for its powder base. This lotion can be mildly astringent, but not nearly so astringent as that which you use with your twice-weekly facial. Witch hazel or toilet water, diluted with water, is delightful, and so is rose water. 

Following this freshening liquid comes your powder base. 

There are many of them on the market, and whether it is a liquid, or a vanishing cream, or any other kind of a cream is entirely up to you.

Just these three things-cleansing, freshening lotion, powder base. Remember them, because each is important. 

And now for cosmetics. These should consist of lip rouge, cheek rouge, if you use it, a brow-and-lash darkener, an eyelid darkener, and face powder. You may not, of course, use all of these things, but I think if you once learn how to use them correctly you will use them, especially at night.

Some Tips on Rouge 

FIRST apply your face rouge, not your lip rouge. Never put it on over powder, especially if you use a paste rouge, which is by far the most natural but the most difficult to apply correctly.

I think this is a good place to tell you who should use face rouge and who should not. A sallow, tanned, olive, or even creamy skin is always flattered by it, especially the first three. A very dead white skin seldom is, and I think that trying to rouge such a skin is like trying to rouge the petals of the magnolia. Blondes must rouge very, 242

very lightly and sparingly, and types with Titian or auburn hair should never rouge at all, no matter what color the skin is. There might be an exception to this, but I have yet to see or even conjure it in imagination. A brunette, however, rouges lightly or heavily according to the shade of brunette she is a golden-brown-haired type rouging more lightly than a black-haired one, especially if the latter has dark skin.

And now, where to put your rouge. First of all, it must as nearly as possible imitate your natural coloring. You have none? You have had, at some time or other. For purposes of experimenting slap your cheeks briskly with flexible patter of whalebone. Wait until the coloring has subsided somewhat, and then see just where it is and rouge accordingly. Apply the rouge to the center of your cheek, then pat and blend it toward the outer edges, so that there is absolutely no definite line where it merges into the skin. 

How to Rouge to Improve

Face Lines 

But you must know how to apply rouge in a way to correct your facial contours as well. An oval face may be made to look unpleasantly long and thin by rouging too high on the cheekbones and too low on the jaws in an up-and-down direction, whereas the same face will look positively lovely when the rouge is applied a bit under the cheekbone and in a circular movement toward the ears. The opposite is true of the too-round face-always apply your rouge to give a longish effect. Put the color emphasis on the cheekbones, and rouge well up into the 243

Lovely Ladies temples, but do not let the rouge be too close to the nose. 

For the broad, large face, put on the rouge with the up-and-down motion, in the direction of the outside corner of the eye, but leaving white space between that and the ears, and between the rouge and the nose on the other side.

A receding chin may be brought into prominence by dabbing a bit of rouge on it, and this you should do, especially if your nose is a bit too large. But if your chin is jutting and aggressive, for heaven's sake don't even touch it with the rouge! 

And Now the

Powder AFTER the rouge comes the powder. Only lovely ladies know how to select the appropriate shade and quality of face powder! More often than not your becoming face powder is one that cannot be found already prepared-so you must buy two shades and mix them yourself, and perhaps two different consistencies of powder and mix them also. A light grade of powder may be too light for your face, and a heavy powder cake unbeautifully, whereas a blend of the two will be just exactly what you want.

And then colors of powder. Why are we so prone to think of face powder only in three shades—white, rachel, and flesh-when there are many shades in between these, and when any one of these three seldom suits any one type? 

Take white powder. There isn't one skin in fifty to which it is actually becoming, for powder must not only 244

match the skin, but it must look as though it were not put on at all. Rachel, the generally accepted powder for a brunette's skin, often, although it does match the skin, darkens it the more. And I have yet to see the woman who can actually wear successfully a pink powder. 

If the woman with a dead-white skin will blend one box of white powder with half a box of pink and half a box of rachel her skin will have the lovely, warm glow of a magnolia petal instead of the waxy whiteness of the lily. A dark skin is flattered by a rachel powder, lightened with flesh powder, or a bit of white powder as well.

These are suggestions if there is no shop accessible where they mix the powder to suit the color of your skin and of the proper consistency. If such a shop is available it will pay you to have them first mix you a sample, which you can try faithfully for a week, and then order a very large box of it, one that will last you for a year.

A Word About 

Eyes Now you have rouge and powder. The next make-up step is your eye-andeyebrow treatment. First brush the powder out of them with your eyebrow brush dipped in waterand in the event that you do not use anything further on them, always do this at least after you have powdered. 

I do think that, when cleverly and sparingly applied, the dark creams one can buy for the eyelids are very effective, especially at night. But unless your skin and hair are very dark use a brown shadow instead of a black, and if you are a blonde use a blue shadow. Not only does this 245

Lovely Ladies little shadow make your eyes seem larger and more soulful, but it actually detracts from any dark circles under them.

Speaking of dark circles reminds me that, should they suddenly appear and persist for any length of time, you should consult a physician immediately, because it may indicate a serious organic trouble, or anemia induced by fatigue. However, this is occasionally a hereditary characteristic and not at all serious. You can eliminate them entirely by rubbing them with the tiniest speck of rouge and then powdering over this. It is the old grease-paint idea, really. 

Brow and lash coloring takes a steady and expert hand, but the results are magical and are well worth many hours of practice. Always use a tiny, stiff brush, like a toothbrush rather than a paint brush, and brush the hairs of your eyebrows with it. Never touching the skin beneath. Then just brush the outer edges of your eyelashes, which are usually not so amply supplied with coloring matter as they are near the roots. Have your coloring matter as dry as it can possibly be and still adhere to the hairs. This will prevent their sticking together and having that "spiked” appearance that is too theatrical to be interesting.

If your eyes are small, or if they are too close together, particularly if they are both, you are justified in darkening the outer corner of each eye with an eyebrow pencil. If your eyes are small, but not too close together, at night you may add a tiny dot of red at each inner corner, which will serve to give them brilliance and enlarge them. 246

Another subtle little secret, which, however, can only be used at night, is that of putting a little vaseline or cold cream on the upper eyelid and just the tiniest speck on the lower lid toward the nose. But put this on carefully and don't overdo it. 

And now your last make-up gesture for the day is your lip rouge. If it is possible, and if you use a paste rouge, let this be the same rouge you use on your face. You can obtain a most delightfully natural effect by rouging lips in the desired shape very heavily and then wiping all of this rouge off with your cosmetic rag, or whatever you use. Remember that lips are rouged to simulate a natural coloring, not as though one were lacquering furniture. This is a fault that is all too common to-day, but certainly no lovely lady would ever be guilty of it.

If your mouth is large concentrate all of your lip rouge at the center of it, and put hardly any at all toward the corners. But if it is small, and especially if it is small and thin, rouge it far out toward the corners and a little above and below the lips themselves.

That is the proper daily aid to beauty, remembering to tone your cosmetics down or accentuate them according to the occasion and the intensity of your own natural coloring. For this reason one should, whenever possible, apply make-up under the same light as that under which it will be worn. If you must make up for the daytime under artificial light, then at least go to the window before you leave the room and inspect yourself carefully, and 247

Lovely Ladies tone down the coloring a bit, because it will in all probability be too brilliant for out-of-doors. 

The will to have, and the courage to keep! Those are the only requisites of beauty to-day. Every woman born can be beautiful with these two. And even she who is born with beauty must keep it these days, or it will go the way of all fragility. 

Be beautiful! Determine it, intelligently set about it. 248

Chapter XI OUR EXERCISES EXERCISE! At the mere mention of the word every little lazy, petted, and pampered muscle and fiber in your body shivers delicately, and murmurs, "But my deah!" 

The funny and also just a little sad part of it is that exercises are not the formidable thing that we are inclined to think they are, and make them out in our minds as being-which is really just a sort of subconscious excuse not to do them. 

But you must understand that you should not exercise too much. This does nothing in the world but overdevelop muscles for which you have no use, actually, and stores up a lot of future grief for you. Ten minutes in the 249

Lovely Ladies morning or at night, whichever you prefer, of musclestretching exercises will give you a graceful carriage and posture and keep you young generally. If only every child were taught the habit of taking ten minutes of the proper muscle stretching and loosening exercises every morning, exactly as he is taught to brush his teeth, this would become such a habit that to start the day off without these exercises would make him feel as uncomfortable as starting the day without washing his face.

There are, of course, all sorts of corrective exercises that eliminate ugliness of the body. Even an exercise done three times every morning will have a beneficial effect within a month. I am not going to give you many of these corrective exercises in this book-perhaps a few suggestions later, that is all. The important thing is to sell yourself the idea of exercising. Until you realize the importance of it, it will only be a burden to you and something that you will commence and then abandon. But when you realize that just ten minutes of very simple exercises every morning, and those performed in your bed at that, will give you not only grace but poise and beauty, how can you let laziness prevent you from taking them? If for any reason you must let one day go by without your exercises, catch up the next day, for two days in succession may be fatal to your habit. Just this little loosening and working of your muscles and increasing of your circulation every morning will put a sparkle in your eye and keep your complexion fresh and young. 250

Our Exercises 

Habit Is the

Important 

Thing 

PROMISE yourself solemnly that you will take ten minutes' exercise once every twenty-four hours for one whole month.

This will be sufficient time for you to establish the habit, and to notice such results that you will have no desire at all to discontinue it. The first week only will take time and patience. That will mean that you are learning the routine, and that you are stretching little muscles that you have allowed to fall into disuse. 

And please get it clearly in mind before you begin that four slow exercises are more beneficial than forty fast ones of the same movement!

I am going to describe to you a number of what I consider the best exercises taken from many different systems of physical culture. Try each one once, so that you can learn exactly how it is done, and then adopt the ones that you feel are the most beneficial for you and arrange to perform them in the most convenient order. Remember that I am suggesting that you do as many as you can in your bed every morning upon awakening, because this will give you more courage than if you had to pop out in the cold room and take your few standing-up exercises first. After you have taken the reclining ones you will have a pleasant glow all over, which will make it less difficult for you to get out of bed for the others. But, if you have the courage to do so, and your figure is inclined to display a feminine but nevertheless aggravating tendency to widen at the hips, take these exercises lying on the hard floor. It will be of help in reducing. 251

Lovely Ladies 

You Don't 

Even Have to

Get out of Bed for These 

THERE goes the alarm clock. You turn flat on your back and commence with this breathing exercise which you do five times. Take a long, slow breath into your nostrils and way down into your tummy, until you have quite inflated it to the utmost limits. Then hold this breath for a second and expel it through your mouth, again very, very slowly. Five of these. Your heart responds joyfully, your blood tingles, and the coldest room has no terrors for you. This is the way that you stop and breathe every time you feel a little breathless after an exercise. 

Then you commence your exercises-as I said, not necessarily in the order named, but as you wish, only establish a certain order of your own, so that it will become an automatic routine for you at the end of a week, one which you indulge in while you are planning your affairs of the day, if you don't want to concentrate on the particular exercise, which is of course more desirable.

Here's one that is perhaps the simplest, and one with which I like to start after the breathing exercise. It stretches the spine, and therefore gives a more graceful and erect carriage. Simply arch your back away from the bed, as a cat does, lying on your shoulders and your buttocks, trying to bring them as near to each other as you can. Arch 'way up, slowly, then fall down again, completely relaxed. Do this four times.

Here's another one, possibly the next, and it will, by 252

Our Exercises tautening them, reduce the muscles of your hips, arms, thighs, and shoulders. Anyway, after the back-arching that you have just done it will feel good to stretch out all over, as though you were an inch worm trying to cover about an inch and a quarter of distance. Now lift one leg as high as you possibly can without allowing the other one to leave the bed, and with your toes pointed; raise both your arms at the same time straight up and stretch, until they reach straight above you, not behind you.

Stretch, reach up, up, up, with your hands and the one leg. In a moment you lower your leg and your arms, still stretched, and then do the same thing over again with the other leg and your arms, four times with each leg, making eight for your arms. Do this, rest a moment, breathe deeply.

For the next in order, if you wish it, because you are lying flat on your back already, simply lower your arms flat beside you. With a quick movement, thrust your legs as widely apart as possible without bending the knees. 

Now keeping them in that position, raise them as high as possible from the bed. A little difficult to do at first, but you can help yourself with your arms. Stretch, bring your legs together sharply, still in the air, and then lower them with your knees still unbent. Do this exercise four times. 

It reduces your abdomen and waist, but mostly it develops a greater ease and smoothness of leg movement for your walking and dancing.

Another one somewhat the same, although not quite the same, and one that you might do next, does the same 253

Lovely Ladies things for your legs and abdomen and also helps your shoulders and arms. You are still lying on your back, stretching all you can, and you have rested a moment since the last exercise, completely relaxed and breathing deeply in the proper way. Now bend your knees and bring them up as close to your chest as possible, keeping your feet together. Then clasp your legs just below the knees with both arms, fingers locked, and pull, slowly, until you feel the strain in your back, hips, thighs, and shoulders. 

Now let go with your hands and throw your feet out and upward quickly, toes pointing. Try to hold this position, and then repeat the exercise. You do this one only three times and you do not let your feet touch the bed until you are through. 

Relax, rest, breathe deeply, and by this time you are quite warm. Now raise your upper body from the waist and try to touch your toes with your finger tips, keeping the knees perfectly straight and the toes pointing, with your head tipped back. This is one exercise that you can do slightly more quickly than the others, and do it six times. You lie flat on your back after each effort to sit up and reach for your toes.

Here comes a harder one, but it does astonishing things to your abdomen, shoulders, and neck. You have rested from your last exercise, so clasp your waist with your two thumbs forward and your fingers spread under the small of your back. Now, always keeping your legs stiffened straight and your toes pointing, raise your feet and legs high, high, to bring your weight on the upper part of your 254

Our Exercises back, balancing yourself by the support of hands and arms. 

Get up as high as you can with your feet, trying to point them straight above you. Now, holding this same position, move the legs briskly as though you were running or bicycling upside down. Lower yourself and relax, and repeat the whole exercise, but only do this a few times. "Wood- Chopping"

Is Beneficial 

Now we jump out of bed and chop wood!

It is a wonderful exercise for almost every part of the body, which must have a certain amount of exercise in order to function properly. Stand with your legs spread apart and your arms straight up and clasped high over your head, as though you were holding a hatchet. Then, whang goes the hatchet down between your feet! It's a very short hatchet, and you give it such a blow that the log of wood cracks right in two and flies across the room. But there are more logs, so you cut up six or eight every morning, and do this a little more rapidly than the exercises when you were simply flexing and stretching your muscles in bed. 

Then, as an improvement on the old trick of stretching over to touch your fingertips to the floor with your feet close together, lean over and touch the toes of your left foot with your right hand, keeping your left arm straight out and behind you while you are doing it. Stand up straight, and repeat the exercise, touching the right foot with the left hand. Do this exercise ten times.

There is one more, an exercise similar to the one you 255

Lovely Ladies have done in bed, but it exercises different muscles if you do it out of bed. Squat to the floor with your knees apart, then hug one knee tightly to your chest, with your hands clasped, not your arms. Pull, then let go, stand up, and relax. Do the same thing with the other knee, and then back again-alternating so that you have done it to each knee about four times.

Then into your warm bath, which is of course not too warm, and you will feel delicious! If you don't feel like attempting a cold shower, and indeed I do not suggest it if you are not already accustomed to it, at least sponge yourself off with considerably cooler water just before you get out of the bath. 

There are your morning exercises. Remember that these are not corrective exercises, but suggestions for a daily routine-beginning now. 

Another thing that is ever so important to health, beauty, and the warding off of old age, is normal elimination, which these exercises will promote. Don't underestimate the importance of this.

Round Shoulders 

THERE may be some corrective exercises that you need, that indeed most of us do need. For example, business women of the less active type frequently have round shoulders! Yet six exercises a morning will correct them in an amazingly short time. 

First, let me tell you that round shoulders come from a stretching of certain back muscles and a tightening of 256

Our Exercises certain muscles in the front, due, of course, to an habitually assumed posture. Simply tighten the back muscles and stretch the front muscles, but the right ones! A spasmodic effort to straighten your posture by throwing your shoulders back doesn't mean anything at all, but proper exercises will effect an improvement in four mornings. 

You stand before a mirror, because what you see in the mirror has a subconscious effect upon you. Keeping your stomach and hips perfectly still, bend your head as far back as you can, describing an arch with the small of your back, but not moving your hips a fraction of an inch from where they are. Do this very slowly until you feel all the muscles across the small of your back contracting. Are you poking your stomach out and moving your hips? It's the upper part of your body only that you move. You do this exercise six times, very slowly. Start the first morning by doing it four times, and work up to six within the next day or two. 

This exercise tightens up those lazy, loosened back muscles, and you must next do one to loosen and stretch the front muscles that have become tightened and are pulling your body down in front. This you do by standing perfectly still and raising, raising, raising the upper part of your chest until you feel that you look either like a pouter pigeon or a caricature of the mode of the Nineteenth Century, sans bustle. You stretch as much as you can, then you relax, and you do it all over again. This one you do six times also, and in less than a month you will notice results, although you must continue the exercises 257

Lovely Ladies until the muscles are thoroughly accustomed to your changed position, so that they will not slip back into their old lazy habits.

Now, just for fun, to see how much you need to straighten your shoulders, and to accustom yourself mentally to a proper posture, stand with your back to the wall -heels, calves, buttocks, shoulders, and back of head touching the wall. Tense your whole body in this position for a moment, and then relax it completely, still in this position. 

Get the sensation of your body in this position. Concentrate, impress upon your subconscious mind the fact that you are going to hold yourself this way. And with no further conscious effort you will! 

IT IS feminine for arms to be soft, but not 

Those Soft too soft-the upper arm especially. Try

Arms a hot epsom salts pack-gauze soaked in the saturated solution of the salts and covered with a waterproof silk, and then a towel-applied to both arms an hour a day. This will give quick results in a week, but not lasting ones. Be sure to wear rubber gloves while doing this to protect your hands from the hot salts, which have an ugly drying effect on them. After you have kept this hot pack on for an hour (perhaps you will have to change it once), if you possibly have someone to help you, do this, remove the waterproof silk and the towel and for about twenty minutes have the gauze dipped in the hot solution and changed as it gets cold. The heat does one thing, and contact with the air does another. 258

Our Exercises 

But for permanent arm beauty and firmness, not to mention its service for reducing, you will have to add the good old "dumbbell" exercises to your morning program. 

Relax your arms, then clench your fists and bring them sharply to your shoulders, flexing your muscles at the same time. Now thrust them out directly in front of you, then above your head, then out at the sides-each one ten times. Each exercise, I mean, not each arm.

One more very important exercise, because it exercises certain other muscles that are not affected by the exercises above, is particularly good for the muscles of the under part of the arm, which are those that actually get the least exercise and need it the most. Stretch your arms out wide on either side, fingers tense, and commence describing a circular motion backward with each arm. 

Do this about twenty times, hang your arms and relax them utterly, and then do the same thing again, but making your circle forward.

This is quite enough. You don't want to develop the muscles too much, only sufficiently to keep them firm and elastic. When you are using ice on your face, or very cold water, use this on your soft upper arm, too. It keeps the muscle and skin tightened. 

For large ankles, that curse of beauty, add this to your morning program: Point your toes without moving the legs, make a circle in the air with your toes, circle toes ten times outward, then ten times inward. And do this every day!

An epsom salts foot and ankle bath will help, too, if 259

Lovely Ladies you must have immediate although not permanent results. 

Follow this always with cold water until your feet are quite chilled, then dry them and put on lots of talcum powder. If you suffer from unpleasant odors of the feet, use powdered boracic acid instead of the talcum. And wear an elastic ankle bandage at night, especially if you always wear low shoes.

99 NO WOMAN can have truly womanly beauty without beautiful breasts. If illness or childbirth causes them to droop, don't allow this condition to continue for a single month. And allow no perverted current ideas to cause you to bind or strap them into a semblance of boyish flatness, and thus break down their muscles and delicate tissues.

First, the undeveloped breasts. Simply massage them in a rotating movement five minutes night and morning, with lanolin or cocoa butter, all that they will absorb. 

Dust with talcum. Always mix a few drops of tincture of benzoin with lanolin or cocoa butter for whitening.

Next, breasts that sag because of illness. Watch this particularly after operations, after childbirth, and after nursing a child. They must have a massage with diluted alcohol, then a good brisk slapping with a folded towel wrung out in cold water, then the upward and rotating massage with the lanolin or cocoa butter, unless they are already very large. Never under any circumstances attempt to reduce your breasts with epsom salts or reducing 260

Our Exercises creams or bandages, or rubbing contraptions which are beneficial to other parts of the body. 

Continue these ministrations until your breasts are beautiful, and return to the measures if your breasts tend ever to lose that beauty.

There is a simple exercise for a firm and beautiful neck. 

Most neck exercises are entirely too strenuous, and actually develop muscles and make the cords and tendons prominent and ugly. The one neck exercise that will not do that is that of rotating your head on your neck, slowly, with all of you perfectly relaxed. Drop your head forward as if it were very, very heavy, roll it sideways and then drop it back, back, back, as though it were still very heavy, then down to the other side, front again, and continue. 

Do this every morning, if your neck is not all that you wish it to be-and do it every morning if it is, to keep it so!

THE old Romans, that beautiful race of courageous souls and well-formed bodies, typified the saying: "A sound mind in a

The old medieval distinction between body and soul no longer holds true. Body is no longer the enemy of souland for that matter never was, except in the minds of perverted æsthetes. A lax, ugly, undeveloped body can never house a beautiful soul, but a sense of physical wellbeing tends to beauty and virtue.

Remember that exercises, corrective and general, are absolutely necessary for balanced circulation; to keep the 261

Lovely Ladies muscles limber, loosened, and thus give you grace and poise; and to keep from muscles and bones that brittleness which is the first sign of age and decay. But remember also that a lovely lady does not need over-developed muscles. 262

Chapter XII EATING WISELY TO RETAIN BEAUTY 

ALMOST every young girl arrives on the threshold of womanhood with a pleasing and lovely body. She is like a butterfly which has sloughed off its chrysalis and is tensing its wings for first flight. Her eagerness to meet life and to accept it adds to the glamor and radiance which surround her. Then she is twenty-five, thirty, thirty-five, forty years old. Frequently her original glamor and beauty has been supplanted by other things-husbands, homes, and children, and in addition to all these she has accumulated flesh, pounds of it. 

Why? 

There is no reason, except that she does not understand a few simple little facts which would enable her to retain a beautiful figure in keeping with her mature years and beauty. Youth passes. It sometimes is a period to hold precious in one's memory. But who wants to remain young forever? Each year brings its store of experiences and its increment of maturity. We grow older and more interesting, but we need not grow less beautiful.

Most women, from the time they are twenty-five to the time they are fifty, gain an average of five pounds each year. Think of it-five needless pounds a year! 263

Lovely Ladies Experiments in Diet 

IF ANYTHING more is needed to impress upon you the extreme seriousness of the matter of eating wisely I wish I could take you to a place which I once visited. It is a laboratory in one of the largest and most famous hospitals in this country, and it is in charge of one of the most eminent food specialists of our time. All manner of experiments are part of the day's work in this laboratory, and among the many animals used for experimentation are hundreds of rats. As unpleasant as the comparison may seem, the rats are best for experiment, because their reaction to diet more closely approximates the human reaction to the same diet than that of any other animal of convenient size. 

By varying the diet of these rats the workers in this laboratory are able to achieve almost any desired result. Making fat rats and thin rats is as easy as rolling off a log. 

Making healthy rats diseased also is easy. But they go further than that. They can, by carefully selecting food for a mother rat, make her become ferocious and destroy her litter. Then, by changing her diet, they can make her care solicitously for her next brood of children.

These experiments are startling and they cannot help 264

Eating Wisely to Retain Beauty but force anyone to sober reflection about food and its importance. Even if you have the most beautiful figure in the world you should be thoughtful of your eating, for temperament and many other matters may be influenced by improper diet.

Genuine Beauty Comes from Within

OUR forefathers thought that the soul had its home in the stomach, and even to-day many savage tribes believe that is true. And in a sense it is. 

To retain one's beauty one must eat wisely. By eating wisely I mean eating the correct foods, and not necessarily abstention from food. And in this day and age particularly there is no reason for anyone's not eating wisely. 

The last ten years have seen a complete revolution in dietetics, the science of food values. To-day we know about food scientifically, and the crimes previous generations perpetrated in the name of gastronomics should be impossible. No wonder that in those days a woman was old at thirty!

To-day we know that embonpoint (once it meant in good condition!) or more vulgarly, fat, is not only not an indication of good health, as our grandmothers supposed it to be, but quite the opposite-an indication of a slow mind and a lazy body. Of course, I am not meaning to include here the few women whose obesity is due to actual physical ailments or morbid glandular structure. 

THE food we eat contains proteins, fats, carbohydrates, mineral matter, and water. Protein builds tissue, repairs 265

Lovely Ladies waste, yields energy, and sometimes helps store fat. 

Eggs, milk, cheese, fowl, fish, nuts, and the leguminous vegetables, such as peas, lentils, beans, peanuts, contain a large percentage of protein. One half of the proteins you eat should be from vegetables rather than

General Information About Food from eggs and meats.

You know when a thing is greasy, fatty, or contains natural oil, as do nuts, cream, and butter. Fats yield energy and are stored as fat.

Carbohydrates yield energy and are stored as fat, but not so readily, and they are found in sugars, though they be grains, syrups, or candies, in starches, in bread, cereals, and some vegetables, and in animal foods. To retain the carbohydrates in vegetables they should not be pared before cooking. And cook them in a very small amount of water which can be saved for soups and sauces. 

Vitamines which strengthen blood and bones are found in green vegetables.

If you are interested in the nutritive values of your food there are books on the subject which you can turn to, even cook books giving you the nutritive value of every recipe. The information above is merely an outline, sufficient for the average person. 

Remember that this is a chapter on how to retain beauty. 

Don't wait until you think you are pleasingly plump to eat sanely and carefully. When you might think yourself pleasingly plump, the other person thinks you stout!

But there is no reason why one should try to achieve 266

Eating Wisely to Retain Beauty a matchlike slenderness. Like all perverted fashion ideas, the craze for matchlike slenderness has come and gone.

It never will be beautiful to be slender to the point of emaciation, nor will it ever be beautiful to be fat. Realize what type of figure you have and keep it normal and healthy. Never try to train it in ways that are unnatural. 

By the time you have reached the age of twenty-five you should know what kind of figure you are inclined to have. 

If you are inclined to be a little overweight, it is far simpler to watch your diet all of the time than to reduce after you have become fat. And it is not difficult to remember that the articles of diet which make out-and-out fat, regardless of their other desirable food values, are sugar, starches, and fats. Cut down on these, try even eliminating them wholly from your diet for a month or two. If you do eat one of them to excess at any time, make up for it by cutting out all fattening things the next day or two. 

We know now that we all eat too much. Only man and domestic animals do this, a wild animal never. Therefore, unless you have been trained by a wise mother from childhood not to eat too much, you should begin to train yourself now. And you should teach your little ones this, too.

They need to eat the amount they do now only for bodybuilding, but after they have reached the age of their full growth they should cut down the amount of food until it conforms to actual bodily needs and no more. Naturally, a man or woman who is very active and leads an outdoor life needs a totally different kind of diet from that of the sedentary person. 267

Lovely Ladies 

Everyday eating should consist of less meat, more vegetables and fruits, less starches and sugars. Meat should never be served more than once a day, if that often, and there should be two vegetables at a meal, as well as a salad. (This means two vegetables in addition to potatoes, if you eat them.)

And why is it that we have such an aversion to cooked fruit? It is in all probability because the fruit isn't cooked properly, for I defy anyone to spurn a dish of those perfectly luscious California prunes. To cook them properly, soak them a couple of hours in cold water, then pour the water off and put them on the stove in very little water, just enough to cover barely the top ones. Then put a tight cover on this, and put it over a very low flame, letting them cook like this for about an hour and replenishing the water if necessary. The slow boiling and steaming process brings out all of their natural sweetness, and you don't use any other sugar. Try it once this way and you'll never again eat the awful concoction swimming in thick syrup that you have heretofore known as "stewed prunes. 99 Fruit is excellent for one, as it contains about all of the minerals you need and acts as a tonic as well. If you can't have fresh fruit, learn to like stewed fruit, as you will if it is properly cooked and not coated with sugar.

The bulk of your diet can well be vegetables and fruit. 

All of us, unless we have learned not to do so, eat too much bread (starch) for the average civilized life that we lead.

As though it were not bad enough to eat too much starch 268

Eating Wisely to Retain Beauty in the form of bread, we eat even more starch in the form of potatoes, spaghetti, and pastes of all sorts, gravy thickened with flour, and of course pastries and cakes. 

This is bad not only from a reducing standpoint, but from the standpoint of dietetics as well. Even if you are underweight, you can never hope to gain by overloading your system with starches.

Information 

About 

Reducing 

You come to realize that you are not only "a little heavy" but positively fat! And then you determine to get it off and keep it off. Under no circumstances allow yourself to become discouraged. You cannot hope to remove in one month or two what it has taken you a lifetime to accumulate. And it is not healthy nor safe to do this drastically. 

The first thing is to shrink the stomach stretched by overeating until it complains if it isn't full of food all the time. First you must discipline it, and this discipline may be as you please. Personally I should much prefer to have three unpleasantly hungry days than a month of days with a little gnawing, insistent hunger. You will, of course, consult your doctor before you attempt this three-day fast, which is exactly what I am suggesting, and he will of course tell you not to do it. They always do. But that isn't what you consult him for. You consult him to find out if there is any actual organic reason why it would be dangerous for you to fast-as, for instance, any organic heart trouble. 269

Lovely Ladies 

I PROMISE you that only three days will be 

It's Not Hard! uncomfortable, and that after they are over the rest of the diet and the gradual, simple reducing of your unwanted flesh will be absolutely painless. You may have to go away from your friends and family for these three days. I think that is a very good idea and one that will help you to keep your mind off yourself. 

Anyhow, make up your mind to the fact and then forget about it. Don't keep telling yourself that you are fasting, that you haven't eaten all day or for two days, or keep watching yourself for signs of weakness or illness. 

You can also achieve admirable results with a three days' orange or buttermilk diet, and this is more satisfying than a complete fast.

Prepare yourself for your fast by taking a good laxative the night before. In the morning upon awakening drink a large cup of hot water and continue drinking water for the entire three days of your diet or fast whenever you feel chilled or a little nauseated or faint. 

I suggest that your three-day fast be in the summertime. By fasting for three days during hot weather you will notice that you do not feel the heat at all, but don't put it off till summer for that reason!

First Day 

Now we have started the first day. The headache that may result comes from faulty elimination and poisons in the system which, like the wild beasts that they are, are only quiet when they are fed. But the laxative you have taken the night before will 270

Eating Wisely to Retain Beauty quite eliminate this for the first day. When mealtime comes around, just forget it. This is an ideal time to go for the brisk walk that you are going to take every day for these three days. 

The next day may be your bad day. Upon awakening you must take an internal bath. And this is the day that your protesting stomach will make the most fuss. No matter if you feel a little tired or weak to-day, go for your walk, drink your hot water, and forget about it. 

The third day you awake feeling strangely better, and your mirror shows you a very gratifying brightness of eye.

You arise and take your few very mild exercises-and by the way, I forgot to tell you that you must cut down on your usual morning exercises throughout these three days and do only the simpler ones that do not overtax your heart, while you must not stop your exercising or under any circumstances stay in bed, no matter how bad you think you feel. You must again take an internal baththis helps you clean out all of the waste from your overloaded stomach and intestines. And it eliminates any headache, which only comes when your stomach generates foul gases, as it does when you have been overeating for any length of time. 

Then you may have another little spell of weakness. Do not forget your walk and your exercises and the hot water. 

You will feel as though you could go on with this fast forever, so little trouble is it on this third day. And you can, if you like, extend it a day. 

The first day that you break your fast you must, of 271

Lovely Ladies course, take it very easy-and the second day as well. 

Otherwise you will have indigestion, possibly very serious indigestion. Start with a dish of stewed fruit that is not too sweet. Now wait for two hours and then take a glass of milk and a couple of graham crackers. For luncheon have a baked apple with a pat of butter on it and some more milk. Two or two and a half hours later have a glass of hot milk, and for supper milk and graham crackers, or milk and toast, with a soft-boiled egg. Perhaps it does not sound like a great deal of food, but you are going to feel that it is when you have eaten it. 

Now your stomach has shrunk to something nearer its proper size. The immediate and gratifying result will be that you will notice this in your abdomen, especially as you are going to begin at once taking quite seriously the exercises that I have told you about elsewhere, which tighten these abdominal muscles. 

Be Careful

About Resum ing Your Diet 

BUT now, after your three-day fast, can you go back to eating the things that you were eating before? Certainly not! In the first place, you are not even going to eat one so-called "regular" meal. This stretches your stomach all at once and then leaves you hollow the rest of the time. 

Instead, you will eat five or six small meals during the day, and therefore will not notice any gnawing or half-satisfied feeling that we strangely enough call "hunger." Thus you will reduce slowly and safely, surely and painlessly. 

When I say five small meals, I mean small ones. And ever 272

Eating Wisely to Retain Beauty and always are you going to be on the lookout for those devastating sweets, that protuberance-producing starch, oils, and fats.

It is the two lumps of sugar, or even one, in your morning coffee that tells the story. You can learn to like your coffee without sugar. In fact, you can't appreciate coffee until you learn to like it without sugar and taste the perfect aroma of its oils and perfume! And then the two teaspoonfuls of cream in each cup, and the pat of butter on each piece of toast, and the sugar used in cooking the stewed fruit. What if your breakfast consists only of fruit, coffee, and toast, when it also includes all of these fat-producing calories? 

Learn to go without them and to like it. You can. Just one month of breakfasts consisting of black coffee, one or even two ooooo pieces of thin toast without butter or jam, and fresh or stewed fruit without sugar will make you wonder how you could ever have endured that sweetish breakfast for all of those years. 

If you don't wish so much breakfast, cut it down to the fruit and coffee, or tea, but establish the habit of eating fruit without sugar every morning.

Between luncheon and dinner drink a glass of milk and 273

Lovely Ladies nibble a graham or wholewheat cracker without butter.

You have no idea how nice this is, seemingly just enough to bridge that gap between breakfast and luncheon.

First let me remind you that the typical American luncheon at a soda fountain or confectioner, is all wrong, even if those delicious sandwiches, that lovely coffee, and those fresh pieces of pie and cake are so good. If you are in business and haven't the opportunity to cook your own luncheon, go to a small restaurant or cafeteria and eat vegetables.

For luncheon you must establish the habit of eating a big salad, without lots of salad dressing. This salad can be quite enough, with your cup of hot tea or coffee; but you may eat more if you wish, depending upon whether your luncheon is your main meal or not. Here are the things that one can safely eat and yet maintain a correct weight. How about copying this little list and pinning it up on the dining-room wall until you have memorized it?

The idea is that once you learn to watch for the fattening things and either cut them out entirely or partake of them moderately, you can get along very well indeed. While you are learning how to do it, like everything else, it is a bit more difficult.

This is for ordinary eating, not for the woman who has allowed fat to pile up to the extent that she must diet rigidly for a year or more. After you have attained your proper weight you must keep it. But that does not mean that all fattening things are cut out of your life forever.
