Since the war we have had an irrefutable example of this all over Europe. Many lovely and cultured women who had known every wealth and luxury were almost or entirely ruined financially by the great upheaval, and yet they maintained the same degree of personal elegance as always.

As a nation we are young, and all young things are guilty of crudities inseparable from the learning process.

Yet, as a nation, our women are considered the bestdressed women in the world, an honor that has for generations belonged to France. And for a nation to have such a reputation denotes a degree of intelligence on the part of its women. Yet there remains much to be done before we can truly claim this honor permanently. And each lovely lady must do her share. For while it is true that the majority of American women are unquestionably better dressed than a comparable number of women in any country in the world, our few élégantes have much to learn from our better-versed European sisters. 

The wearing of clothing primarily is a necessary protection against the elements, but, while still more or less fulfilling this need, it also has become an art. But it is an art based upon the intelligent planning and reasoning 467

Lovely Ladies of the individual, and is not merely the blind adoption of some other person's ideas. 

A lovely lady will study the art of expressing beauty and individuality in her clothing because it is an inclusive and irrefutable indication of her intelligence. 468

Chapter III WISE PURCHASES AND WARDROBE ECONOMY 

Jane 

WHAT woman is not interested in wardrobe economy? Inborn in almost every human heart is the desire to get the most for the money we spend, whether this be a matter of necessity or not. 

However ample be your checkbook, the more value you get from what you spend, the more you can spend! 

Wise purchases are the very first thing. The little mother, possibly raising a sizable family on a very small income, with the house to be paid for, the children's schooling, and the car to be bought if possible, has a perfectly natural desire to appear at her best. And when she does purchase something it must be the very wisest purchase she can possibly make.

Business women, for a different reason, must be wise in their purchases. Their reason is mostly one of time-the lack of time in which to shop. Therefore, their purchases must be good and have some lasting, economical merit. 469

Lovely Ladies 

A wise clothing purchase is the garment that you select in color and style appropriate to you as an individual and one that fits into the rest of your wardrobe, without necessitating, consequently, an entirely new set of accessories. A lovely lady must know what she wants and why.

If you depend upon a saleswoman's advice you may expect to be dressed according to the saleswoman's taste. Not that her taste may not be excellent, as she has an opportunity to study the mode trends that you have not. But too often even the fairest-minded saleswomen are intent upon "turning over stock" or "getting rid of deadheads." 

Then, after your wise purchase, comes your very first wardrobe-economy hint. This is neatness. A shoe or gown carefully cared for will long outlast one that is not. 

True, one can have a garment cleaned as often as one wishes and can afford it, but does not the cleaning in time rob it of its newness? And why-when a little attention to the wearing and the hanging of it will save innumerable cleanings?

Padded clothes hangers and dress covers that take but a moment to make are indispensable to both neatness and economy. Shoe trees will prolong the life of shoes, both in appearance and wear. Gloves cleaned at home will outlast those sent to the cleaner's. That rubbed and slightly oily back edge of your hat brim makes it look shabby before it really is; a bit of cleaning fluid and cotton will refurbish it instantly. A little fine comb will give fur collars and cuffs miraculous new life, and there are innumerable ways to prolong the life of silk hosiery as well as lingerie. 470

Wise Purchases and Wardrobe Economy 

Ensembles for

Economy 

IT WAS the war-impoverished gentlewoman who inaugurated the ensemble idea, which has since taken such a hold on the fashionable world. She who before had always plenty of money to spend for her clothes knew well that in order to hold her position in life a woman must present a smart outer covering to the world! So she began by selecting a coat that could be worn with two frocks; each outfit seemingly an entirely different one. Then the idea spread to three- and four-piece suits, until now it is possible for one to have, say, a reversible plaid sports coat and six or eight sports dresses with accessories each of which makes a new outfit and utilizes the same coat.

very clever idea that had quite a vogue a few seasons ago, but which is fashionable at any time, is that of the sleeveless dinner dress which can have an accompanying jacquette with sleeves, this making it a very smart daytime dress. It is perfect for the afternoon bridge party which merges into a "Let's go to the Rose Room for dinner and dance."

A smart skirt on a sleeveless tunic top can be the foundation for innumerable blouses and tunics. A jacquette, either hip length or three quarters, of the skirt material lined with a matching crêpe accompanies such a skirt. 

For a street outfit, just the jacquette and skirt serve; for a wrap, the jacquette may be worn with a tunic which is over the skirt, or it is smart as a separate jacquette with other outfits. 471

Lovely Ladies 

Economy in

Accessories It is never economy to have only one pair of hose, one pair of gloves, or one pair of shoes. Two will outlast three or four pairs worn consecutively, so it is well worth the effort in the beginning to have a change. Hosiery economy consists of buying two pairs of exactly the same hose and washing each pair after it is worn once, in warm water and very little soap-this only at the feet. Always turn your hose wrong side out when you wash them, always rinse them well, and always hang them in a shaded place, never in the sun. Buying two pairs that match will make it possible for you to have an extra when one rips—and it is seldom that both stockings wear out at the same time. 

Two pairs of shoes will allow you to alternate, thus letting one pair have the benefit of drying and straightening on shoe trees and the little cleansing touches that prolong their life. If you turn your heels over, rubber heels are far more economical than leather ones.

One can do with one smart bag, well kept. If you would keep your bag for any length of time, don't stuff it full of things that will stretch it out of shape. Buy a conservative shape and color, of which you will not tire, and keep it cleaned with a bit of cotton and cleaning fluid. 

One Hat a 

Year? Yes! 

CAN a woman really wear one hat the year round, and seem always chic? Yes!

For if the hat is becoming in the first place, of a conservative style that will not be affected by radical style changes, of an expensive make and good material 472

Wise Purchases and Wardrobe Economy that is not seasonal but general-such as fabric instead of straw-it might have been purchased yesterday instead of yesteryear.

Were one to purchase a coat, knowing that that coat would have to do for every occasion and perhaps many seasons, I would suggest a very good coat, not necessarily expensive, but with excellent wearing qualities. This coat should be in the simple, straightline style, of a becoming and general color, and not a sports coat, because it is permissible to wear a dress coat at any time but not a sports coat. With care, relining, possibly dyeing and recollaring, this coat can be made to last indefinitely. A further purchase should be made, though, if possible—an inexpensive sports coat for general wear, to save the other one.

Real economy demands only one or possibly two colors in one's entire wardrobe, with the exception of dresses for evening and house. Dark blue or black, and either gray or beige are preferable. But should one have black, gray, and beige, it would not be so easy to maintain a smart appearance at all times. It is well to center on one general color scheme, then build your wardrobe around that.

Evening Wear 

FOR a woman who attends few evening functions a black chiffon dress worn over a flesh-colored slip is ideal, inasmuch as it demands black satin pumps which she can wear during the day, and the dress might be made to accommodate any formal afternoon tea. A shawl makes the loveliest as well 473

Lovely Ladies as most economical wrap, as it too might be used for other occasions. Nothing that stays in the closet is real economy!

Made-overs and Chic

Touches 

A SIMPLE matter of turning a material inside out will often produce an entirely new garment. And dyeing is an art, not a necessity. Do you realize that the famous couturiers dye new fabrics in order to obtain all shades, the very off-shades which they promote and capitalize for a season or two? 

Economy Is 

Common Sense

ECONOMY is merely using common sense! 

The woman who plans her wardrobe in detail instead of buying this, that, and the other thing that strikes her fancy, will always be chic with very much less strain on her pocketbook and disposition. 

Plan carefully, buy wisely, take care of your things, avoid fads and extremes, and you will always be attractively dressed with the minimum of expense. Realize that elegance is a necessity of the age and an indication of your mentality. Yet realize also that it is no longer the primary consideration in a woman's life, and it must be acquired and kept with the minimum of energy. 474

Chapter IV 

OUR CAREERS 

EXCEPT in unusual situations where the case is altered by circumstances, a career is a most important consideration for the modern woman who would be well balanced, interesting, really worth while, and of course lovely. 

A career or a hobby, whichever you please to call it, is anything constructively interesting that will keep you interested and consequently interesting. 

All intelligent modern girls are faced with a problem between the time of finishing school and the time of marrying. What are you going to do with the weeks and months, 475

Lovely Ladies and even possibly the years, before you take that thrilling march to the altar? Perhaps your graduation marks the close of a really bitter struggle which your parents have had to put up to furnish you with the priceless boon of education. (And don't ever think that all of your education is to be had on the campus, for that's only the beginning of it!) It is time now for you to repay them slightly, and in a material way, for their sacrifices for you. 

Or, taking another example, perhaps your parents do not need your financial assistance, but you yourself want something to do. You can't just fritter your time away on bridges, and dances, and pink teas, and become a drone in this amusing little beehive of life. So what, I repeat, are you going to do?

The Places for Your Career 

REALIZE that your career will occupy two important periods in your life-before marriage and after motherhood. And one must not entirely overlook the fact that in these extraordinary days it is quite possible that you may not actually find the man you want to marry. This doesn't mean that you don't want to marry. It simply means that incomprehensible Fortune, working through the veil of mystery which we humans have yet to penetrate, guides your steps in one path and those of your proper husband in another. The paths do not meet, and hence, being too strong a woman to marry merely for outward form and convention, you remain single.

Bearing this in mind, you should be that much more 476

Our Careers interested in your career, that much more interested in making your first job not only a job, but a stepping stone toward an interesting and worth-while occupation. 

With your degree from Vassar, or Smith, or Whozis 

Seminary tucked cockily under your arm, you sally forth to look Old Man World smack in the face and "tell him a thing or two." That usually is the attitude of our graduates and, after all, wнO shall say that they are wrong? For the high hope and self-confidence of youth are a rare touchstone of potent possibilities. In another chapter I have discussed the Business Woman and have no wish to be redundant. But I do want you to understand that, even if you take a very small position in the business world, you must give your very best self to it. Only in that way can your work be interesting to you and actually in your own interests.

Your work cannot be interesting unless you go at it honestly. If you are already engaged, expecting to marry soon, and when married to leave the world of business, this is no reason why you should take the first job that is offered just because it pays you an attractive salary, even though you are keeping out of it some woman who not only needs it more than you do, but who will stay with it longer and, in the long run, make a better job of it. When you select a position be fair to all concerned. 

If you are taking only a short flier into the business world (something of which you can never be so very sure, remember), then don't you think, in fairness to other 477

Lovely Ladies women in business, in fairness to yourself, and in fairness to your employer, you should select a position that is by nature temporary? 

One thing, however, you should be sure to consider.

It is the possibility of returning to your job after you have left it. For this reason, and many others, careers in the arts and professions are the most attractive. If you are going to work for two or three years and then marry, you may, after motherhood and when the children are old enough to care nicely for themselves, wish to return to your career.

In fact, you should do just that. So, if you can, why not select some occupation which offers you that opportunity? 

THIS leads me to another point. The arts 

Be Careful of and sciences, of course, offer the greatest

Your Motives attraction as careers. I should not need to point out why that is so, but I do want to give just a little twist to the rudder before you hoist your sails and embark gloriously upon this sea. 

Try to look at your job as a stepping stone to a career.

Be careful of your driving power. Be careful of the impulses which spur you onward. The wild scramble to achieve preeminence in any field, just so people will admire you, and for the monetary rewards which are presumably to be found at the top, is not nearly so fine a motive as a true desire to achieve something beautiful and fine for yourself and for the benefit of humanity. All the praise in the world and all the money you may gain through excelling are not worth the real, honest joy of achievement which will be 478

Our Careers yours only if you undertake your profession honestly and idealistically. 

Granted that a profession is an interesting thing. How does it gear with the rest of your life—with marriage and motherhood, the centerpiece of your life, as it were? It gears admirably. In fact, marriage has much less chance for real success without a career as an additional interest. 

Now you may have been reading this so far and saying to yourself: "Yes, yes-that is right, and that is right, and it's all very nice, but it doesn't apply to me! I don't want to get married.”

Wait a moment. Every normal woman has the fundamental and essential urge toward motherhood, and motherhood to-day means marriage. If you will dig deeply enough into your own mind you will find a real desire to fulfill your biological obligation to life by reproduction, and your sociological obligation by establishing a home. 

Elsewhere in the book this is pointed out to you. We have discussed both of these points at some length, and I want now only to remind you that in one of these chapters I wrote: "A woman need not make the care and raising of her children her entire life."

This is true. There is room for many things in your life, and certainly there is room for the two most important things your home and your career. The day has passed, thanks to mechanical invention, when an intelligent woman says, "My home is my career!" So don't try to cover your subconscious fear that love and marriage will 479

Lovely Ladies pass you by with a defiant statement that you don't want to marry, and give as an excuse for not wanting to marry your interest in your career.

And now for the woman who is already married. 

Are You Keep ing Pace with

Life? 

LIFE moves at a terrific clip-a breath taking gait. And while you are a "youngster" you help it to move. In that fact lies one of life's cleverest little deceptions. You are moving along with life, and there is no mean background against which you can measure the rate of your progress. Hence, you sometimes forget that you are moving. You look around and say, "How peaceful, and calm, and placid is life," when all the time you're rushing along like a whirlwind.

Then you marry, and for many years, it seems, your life is chiefly your own little home and your prescribed circle of friends. But what about Friend Hubby-and what about Henry Junior? He's eighteen years old now, and he's doing the most incomprehensible things—he has the strangest interests. You can't exactly understand them, but you're proud of him. So you sit back, fold your hands, and bask for awhile in the warm sunshine of-stagnation! 

Don't do it! Henry Senior and Junior have interests which you don't exactly understand because they have kept pace with life and you have not. They are both young in spirit. Henry Senior has been out fighting the world for years, and moving with it; Henry Junior is one of those little things which make life move so swiftly. 480

Our Careers 

Be careful-you are being outdistanced. They are leaving you behind, and the mere fact that you listen to their bubbling enthusiasms with calm toleration doesn't mean a thing. You've got to jump right in and find out what it's all about. Don't try to excuse yourself on the grounds that your duties as a wife and mother have "cramped your style." Your husband and your son have had opportunities to keep pace with life which you think you have not had, but actually they have had no opportunities which you could not have taken.

But for the moment that does not alter the fact that they have progressed farther than you have, nor does it alter the fact that you must catch up with them.

Perhaps you've been keeping pretty well up with things by reading "when you had the opportunity." All right. 

But now Henry Junior isn't such a care. You've done a pretty good job in raising him, and he's facing life a pretty self-reliant young man. So just put on the old bonnet, brush off that professional air you had when Henry Senior first came along and set your heart a-thumpin', and skeedaddle downtown. 

Ease Yourself

Back into the Professional 

World 

IF YOU'VE lost any important contacts try to reestablish them. Make new ones. 

Don't be in too big a hurry, but just insinuate yourself back into the professional and artistic life which you left for your job of motherhood. That job's done now, but there's no joy in resting—that is, not the kind of resting which lets the 481

Lovely Ladies rest of the world ripple by you and leaves you a hopeless back number.

A career will revive you. A career, not just a job, unless this job is a stepping stone to a successful career. You need a career. It lies ahead of you, and if you've passed through the experience of motherhood, why then you're just that much better fitted to tackle the most serious sort of a career. 

Don't ask anyone else what you should do. That is something that you must decide for yourself, and never has a woman faced a greater range of choice than she does in this emancipated age. Write, paint, go in for business administration, become a financial expert, try socialservice work-in short, do anything that interests you. But do it seriously and give it all you've got.

You can't afford to stagnate mentally, and that's what you'll do if you give over to that natural impulse of inertia which comes along about the time your little family is beginning to feel its wings and leave the maternal nest. 

Don't tell yourself that you've "earned a rest." What you've really earned is the right to live more completely and fully, and if you don't, you'll be robbing yourself first, and then all the people with whom you come in contact, and who have a right to expect you to be an interesting person, a well-balanced person, and a complete person. 

Résumé 

So IF you're a "youngster," just starting out, look around you and choose a satisfactory career-one which will truly interest you, and one in which you may hope really to do something. Then 482

Our Careers start to work on the foundation. Youth's work is foundation work, and you can't build a beautiful monument on shifting sand. Work at your career, and when the call to marriage comes along, answer it joyfully. 

Then, with marriage and motherhood behind you, go back to your career. If you've been a wise woman you haven't entirely lost contact, and it won't be especially hard to "pick up and carry on." Do so, for the greater glory of yourself, womanhood, and the human race! 483

Chapter V TO THE SCHOOLGIRL 

PERHAPS you will notice that throughout this book I keep suggesting that you teach Little

Daughter or Little Sister the many things that I hope you yourself have learned from these pages. And Daughter or Sister is never too young to be started in the paths of loveliness and beauty.

Habit is so important, and the habit that the child forms when she is five or six years old is much more likely to stay with her than the one she learns some years later. 

The normal child takes almost everything for granted. 

Dad and Mother are the strongest, wisest, and best people in the world, and everything that they say is so. But during adolescence the child awakens to herself; she becomes amazingly aware of herself as an individual and as a member of society. She is undergoing a rebirth-a physical and an emotional one. All sorts of perplexing problems keep turning up-problems that Dad and Mother somehow can't solve because they have forgotten their own adolescence. 484

To the Schoolgirl 

And these problems are important to her, though they seem humorous to older people. The psychologists will tell you that it is during adolescence the young person really forms herself, and that her whole future happiness depends upon answering successfully the questions that arise during that period.

So the rest of this chapter is addressed to the girl from ten to sixteen. to Paris 

HELLO, little lovely lady! Let us have a

A Quick Trip little talk, exactly as though you had journeyed appartement in Paris, where we could be talking while we looked out over tops of green trees and saw thousands of swallows curving and swooping in the air, a fraction of an eye wink beyond them the 

Eiffel Tower, and one eyeball roll to the right the peaceful, never-motionless Seine. 

Rivers always remind me of loveliness-for loveliness goes on and on, centuries after centuries, quietly, peacefully but surely, never stopping its endless flow, even though at times seemingly icebound, or at other times lost in the dense shadows made by overhanging trees. 

You to-day are the lovely lady of to-morrow. And if you are intelligent and modern you will see to it that you develop beauty and loveliness, whether anyone else does it for you or not. Charm, loveliness, and beauty, as they are interpreted to-day and understood by modern women, are all acquired, and any young girl can have them. 

You are beginning to question many things about you- 485

Lovely Ladies manners and customs which the older people seem to accept without question. And you wonder whether they are as right about these things as they seem to be and as you once thought them. Everyone around you says that you are at the "awkward age." Doesn't it make you wild?

Maybe our mothers and grandmothers had a so-called "awkward age," but intelligent young women of to-day do not have! Far from being awkward, this is the beginning of the most exciting period of your whole life, not merely a time which you have to endure patiently until you become, you have no idea how, the lovely lady that you wish to be.

That 

Important Schooling 

THE most important part of your life at the moment seems to be school. Perhaps you've wondered why school is at all necessary. You're learning lots of "stuff," you call it, for which you can't see any earthly use.

In school, first of all, you learn tiresome facts, but these facts help you realize what part you are to play in the world. They are going to help you to live-in one sense by outfitting you with the means of making your livelihood in later life, and in another by giving you a proper understanding of life. 

Another thing, you learn at school to use your mind.

Even the most intelligent person has to learn how to think clearly and economically. You may possess an excellent and very efficient tool, but you may not know how to use it.

There are many things that tend to take your attention 486

To the Schoolgirl away from your studies. There are movies and parties and this, that, and the other thing. I am not suggesting that you become a "grind," that person with her head always in a book, looking so unlovely and so unattractive. I want you to keep the proper perspective on things about you. 

The "grind," you see, has lost her perspective entirely. She is laying too much emphasis on things which should teach her to be a charming and lovely lady, and she's evidently having a great deal of trouble learning. 

In order not to be a "grind" it isn't necessary to be what is commonly meant by a "good sport." Real sportsmanship is a very valuable trait to possess, but you will find that a sportsman and a "good sport" are totally unlike.

Taking a surreptitious pull of nasty throat-burning liquor may make you a "good sport" in the eyes of your escort, but what in heaven's name is sportsmanlike about it? Sportsmanship means fairness and not crying when it hurts. It never means doing foolhardy things or acting against your better judgment. The next time someone says to you, "Aw, come on, be a sport," just answer, "I do consider myself a sport; but doing that isn't my idea of sportsmanship." You win right there. There isn't any comeback to that. 

Acquire Your

Own Code 

THAT is something to think about-good sportsmanship. Acquire a code for yourself as soon as you can about what is and what isn't "sporting"; some people might call it an ethical code, but you needn't be afraid of the word; an ethical 487

Lovely Ladies code is a code of living, and that is what a sporting code is too, isn't it? Be sporting-be fair and don't whimper, and you will find that not only do you not lose in popularity but you will gain a great deal. 

Remember that you are at an age now which is the making of you. You are preparing yourself for life, and the best preparation is to begin living that life now. Forget that you are still growing up and that womanhood is still around the corner. To become the lovely lady that you wish to be you must begin developing yourself immediately in the right direction. You must assist Nature, regulate and fashion yourself into the sort of woman that you admire. 

First of all, I want you to read the first part of this book thoroughly. It is for you especially, and not only for grownup ladies. It is for you simply because it is much easier for you to understand and appreciate than it is for older girls who have already formed habits and ideas that are quite contrary to those that a lovely lady should have.

Perhaps you have been taught all of these things that you have read in the first part of the book, and perhaps not. 

It shouldn't bewilder you to find that Mother doesn't understand these things at all. Every generation changes its ideas just a bit about life, and your mother, though the best in the world, has become accustomed to the ideas of her generation, which, if it didn't think the desire to be lovely and charming almost a sin, did think that charm came to you almost as a gift, that you couldn't learn to be lovely and charming. And that is exactly what I am saying -that you can learn. 488

To the Schoolgirl 

If you haven't been taught all these things, you are going to begin right now and teach them to yourself. Notice in the women about you, that you admire and can't help liking, how they express these things in their behavior. Being a good sport without being rowdy-dow about it, being personally dainty without being fussy, having a sense of humor, being polite and gracious with poise, not poseall of these traits that you admire in some woman you know you may possess yourself.

The chapter on Beauty I want you especially to read. Acquire the 

Exercise Habit

ALSO read the chapter on Exercise. Per haps you have not been taught the importance of daily morning exercises and the fun of them. Right now you are going to start them!

Turn this book to the chapter on Exercises, prop it up on your dressing table, and go through the lessons, one after the other, just as they are written. And then to-morrow morning do those that you like the best, and those that you think you are going to need the most. Do them just a little, don't overwork yourself to such an extent that your muscles are all stiff or sore, but get into the habit of doing them and do them for the rest of your life. 

See that bent, wavering old lady over there, with her face like a dried prune? You don't think you could ever be like her, do you? Well, you are absolutely right-you never could and you never will be like her, because you are going to keep yourself lovely and supple and graceful until you are much, much older than she is, by the almost ridicu- 489

Lovely Ladies lously simple method of taking only eight to ten minutes' exercise every morning! But they must be the proper exercises and taken in the proper way. 

And then remember the daily care of your skin, hair, hands. Don't think for a moment that just because you are young and lovely now that you need not take care of them. 

You need to because it is much better to really keep these lovely things that you have than to bring them back again after they have once gone. I wish that I could impress this upon you. 

Will doing these things take a lot of your time? Will they be hard to do? Will they be tiresome? Indeed they will not! They will be great fun, and they will take only a few minutes after you have really learned them and incorporated them into your daily routine, and in no time at all you will wonder how you ever could have got along without them.

Of course, the information in the Beauty chapter about facials, and things like that, is not for you. But the daily care of your skin and the daily care of your hair, nails, and hands-these are. 

Look for Your Defects 

AND another thing that you must realize while we are having this little talk is that now is the time to correct any little defects that you see in your figure or beauty—as well, of course, as in your character or personality. By defects I mean such things as round shoulders, or ankles that are a bit too big, or a bad complexion. Neglecting them, suffering because 490

To the Schoolgirl of them, wishing they were different is not going to help them a single speck, now or any other time. But the least little bit of attention to them now, before your bones and muscles become really hardened and stubborn, is going to correct them, and they will stay corrected forever.

If you study, understand, and then put into practice all of the things that are suggested in the first part of this book, it won't matter how many or how few pretty clothes you have. For this, I do believe, is the terror of all terrors in our school days. 

And just think a moment Perhaps others wear nicer clothes than you do, have more of them. But, are they in good taste? Do you know what good taste in your school clothes is? You must learn this sooner or later. Why not now? For with your school clothes you can express your good taste and your intelligence, and the same thing will be reflected in all of your clothing for all of your life. The 

Importance of 

Good Taste 

I KNOW you love that party frock of yours, with its cunning little silver belt, its beads and tulle. And that Sunday dress, with its big funny sleeves. But no nice person, really, is going to wear them to school. Others might, perhaps, but not you. 

And therein lies your strength, even if Daddy can't afford to let you have all the pretty clothes that you would like. Because school clothes, no matter how many or what they cost, are strictly limited to sports wear! Only for the 491

Lovely Ladies occasional school party or something of the sort can you wear anything else.

On the separate skirt-and-blouse idea you can build the prettiest and most enviable wardrobe in the school. The difference between you and all the other girls will be that you will plan your clothes while they just sort of buy theirs. 

In the following chapter for college girls you will find out more about this particular kind of a wardrobe. So now 

I want to remind you of the importance of taking care of the clothes that you do have. If you get the habit of doing this you will realize in time not only that it is quite easy to do, but that all the time that you are wearing the clothes they will look nice, you will consequently feel nice in them, and they will last much longer. 

En Route to Lovely

Ladyhood 

Examine yourself. Are you going to be a lovely lady when you grow up? You are! 

You are starting to-day to acquire all the characteristics outlined in the first part of this book and to throw away the childish habits that are not in keeping with your age. And you are starting to take the greatest care of the beauty that you have, correcting and molding little things to suit yourself, not forgetting your exercises and your diet.

Your diet, by the way, you have to watch just as carefully and just as surely as does anyone else. You know it is unpleasant and uncomfortable to be a "fatty," and you never can be really happy as one. Fortunately, we realize at last what it is that makes and keeps one a "fatty"! 492

To the Schoolgirl 

Habits, habits, habits. You are going to form them now, while the process is simple, and keep them all your life. 

There is not one single thing that cannot be yours if you consciously and determinedly go after it, if you intelligently plan and persistently execute.

Chapter VI AND THEN COLLEGE 

So you are going to college!

You have equipped yourself mentally and physically for the part that you are going to play in the scheme of things by attention to earlier sections of this book. Now suppose that we just talk over our college wardrobe. If this is your exciting first year this matter is worrying you, and even if it is your second or third year you had best check up with me and be absolutely sure that you have everything that you will need, and the most that you can possibly get out of your allowance.

With careful planning and scheming beforehand you can have a very attractive and varied wardrobe at a minimum cost that will not sink Father's purse nor eat up your allowance at one terrific gulp. And you have no idea how the right clothes will help you to hold your head high among the sophisticated and well-dressed upper classmen! 

If this is your first year you will be so busy the first few weeks and months adjusting yourself to your work and surroundings that you should not have any worries about your clothes.

THE ideal outfit for the classroom and campus, for any schoolgirl of any age, and one that lends itself to innum- 494

And Then College erable variations, starts with a good-looking coat of the so-called sports variety; then a well-fitting and comfortable skirt which can be worn with sweaters and jacquettes on cooler days and blouses and tunics on warmer days; then good-looking sports shoes, tiny, chic sports hat, gloves, bag, and hose.

Campus and Classes 

The clever little lady will have her coat matched with shoes, gloves, bag, and hat. And the coat will be smart and rather expensive. Then the skirt and one blouse and one sweater will match or harmonize with the coat and accessories -all browns and tans, for instance, or all grays, or grays and blues, or greens and beige. With this initial ensemble with its harmonizing accessories you may have as many colored scarfs, sweaters, blouses, and hats as you wish. Surely you can see how a chic red hat and jacquette worn with the same skirt, coat, and accessories would give you a complete change of attire? This would be with the gray or brown ensemble only, of course. 

It is simply amazing, the number of changes you can achieve with an outfit like this! For example, for outdoor sports you can add a colored suède leather jacket (a bit expensive, but it will last forever) and wrap a woolly scarf 495

Lovely Ladies around your neck, and you will be quite as jaunty as any lady out of the fashion magazine!

A Dressier 

Outfit It is not absolutely necessary to have a dressier outfit, but it will add so to your comfort and peace of mind. Afternoon affairs do crop up, and holidays when you will be glad to shed your working school togs and dress up a bit, perhaps for a run to town and a matinée. It isn't so hard to manage this dressier outfit, if you simply have one smart dress of the not-too-dressy afternoon type, and a dress coat, both matching or harmonizing with your first accessories. Shoes, hose, gloves, bag, and hats are the little things that eat up the budget, you know-and a fancy pin in one's sports hat will often make a dress hat of it without anyone being the wiser!

Possible 

Necessities 

How maddening it is to think you have everything you could possibly need and then to find that you must have a complete swimming or tennis outfit, for which you made no provision in your budget! Try to find out what sports you will be obliged to participate in, or will wish to participate in, and be sure you have everything necessary for them, and try to make what you buy fit somewhat into your present wardrobe. "I HAVE nothing to wear!" This is a sad college refrain when another dance or party comes along and the frantic co-ed goes through her wardrobe and finds that the one- 496

And Then College and-only evening dress has a spot on it, or the scarf belonging to it was badly torn at the last party. 

Party Frocks and Wraps little gayer in

It is best to plan, if you possibly can, to have at least two party frocks-one black, for somewhat informal wear, and one a color, for more "special" occasions. Of course, you can use as many more as you can affordbut if you are one of those fortunates who is not limited to a clothing budget beware displaying poor taste with frocks that are too expensive or too elaborate. 

If you can plan to wear the same black satin pumps for both dresses, that will be fine. If not, sometimes a pair of gold or silver pumps, if your black dress is worn over a gold or silver slip or sports a lamé sash, will do for both frocks. And should you be decided enough in your coloring you can own and wear successfully a pair of those gorgeous brocaded slippers. These do manage to harmonize with every kind of party frock save the pastel ones. 

And haven't you an old evening dress or maybe an afternoon one that can be de-sleeved, dyed black, and then given a new grasp on life with a bit of silver cloth or a bright bunch of flowers? This is one of the tricks of a clever little mademoiselle whom I know, and you would be astonished at the number of good-looking dresses that she has accumulated by this process. 

Be careful to keep at least one pair of fine silk stockings to wear with your party frocks, and never cheat yourself by borrowing them to wear with your daytime clothes. 

Shoe straps pull them and make them look shabby, and 497

Lovely Ladies often the dye comes off dark shoes onto light hose, just enough to spoil them for light pumps. One night you will find yourself without a presentable pair-and who wants to lend silk stockings! 

And remember, a really lovely lady washes her evening hose the day after the party, oh, so carefully, in tepid water and just the least bit of soap on the feet only-—and then puts them away in a soft scented box, so that they won't come into harsh contact with all the other things in the dresser drawer. Here they are always instantly findable.

Last of all is your evening wrap, which you must have. 

You will be altogether uncomfortable without one, and so happy with one, that one will be well worth its original investment.

And not only that, but it need not cost as much as you think. Surely this is one thing that you can make for yourself, and make very nicely, too. An evening wrap of the scarf or cape variety, made of a nice brocaded material, and lined with a sheer material so that the wrong side of the design can show through, is very attractive. You will not have so many uses for it that you will tire of it, and you can simply line it and possibly interline it also with heavier material for the winter. 

That completes your wardrobe. You will need scarcely anything more, and I should certainly prefer to save some of my allowance for the things that are continually cropping up than spend it all at once on clothes. If these few things are selected with attention to your figure and type of coloring you will have the satisfaction of knowing 498

And Then College that you are dressed becomingly and in perfect taste, and that you are equipped for any occasion that might arise. 

With such a wardrobe, and all of the other things that you, being a lovely lady, know and practise, your college days are going to be full to the brim with happiness and interest, and they will remain in your memory as the happiest days of your life. 499

Chapter VII WOMEN IN BUSINESS 

WOMAN has come into her own! 

One finds her clacking away at a typewriter or sitting with the board of directors; managing a restaurant, tearoom, or bookshop; in the newspaper and advertising office; even at the "mike" in the radio-broadcasting studio. She has proved herself. During the Great War she showed the world that for certain kinds of work she was more efficient, more capable even, than her brother. For "contact" work, so necessary to big business, work which demands the type with a "pleasing personality," who must have elegance and charm of manner, our modern miss has proved herself admirably fitted. And it is not at all strange (and why should it be?) that the more lovely is our lady, the more successful a business woman she is. 

Here's to the business woman, God bless her! Who 500

Women in Business could help raving over this interesting product of the Twentieth Century, this fine, bright, alert person, who tackles her job with an enthusiasm envied by men, masters it and more than makes a success of it, and by so doing lays a sane foundation for her career of marriage and motherhood? Was there ever a successful business woman who did not make as successful a wife and mother? No!

Our Careers EVERY modern woman should decide upon some career or hobby early in lifepreferably something which she does not have to relinquish entirely during the years she must devote to bearing and raising her children.

Your chances of success are greatly augmented if you select a career that will interest you. No woman can have any kind of success without working for it, and women are temperamentally unfit to toil arduously at something in which they are not interested. And remember that for a woman to achieve any kind of success at all to-day she still has to work harder than her husband in order to get as far!

Business 

Sportsmanship 

THERE are two kinds of business women. 

One takes her career seriously and is determined really to make a success of what she is putting her time to. The other one is working in order to have something to do, and perhaps to have a little extra money to spend on clothes and luxuries. Her work, therefore, cannot be of the best. The latter type of 501

Lovely Ladies business woman displays a sad lack of sportsmanship. 

If one could possibly be in a business that involved only one's own time and money, then of course it would not matter at all just how much time and effort one put into it. 

But it is only in the very rarest instances that this is possible. For the most part, one accepts a position and tacitly agrees to fill it to the best of her own ability. It does not matter whether the employer is a person or an organization. 

That is sportsmanship in business. If your best, for any reason such as lack of training or uncongenial environment, is not the best that could be found for that position, sportsmanship would demand that you either acquire the training or relinquish the position to someone who is better equipped to handle it. There is a place in the world for everyone.

And then, to put it more selfishly: while you are working and spending those eight hours a day at a job you don't care for, might you not better be improving yourself, studying and aiming for a higher type of position than the one you now hold? The results from so doing will be all in your favor. After all, you may not find just the man you wish to marry, and after ten years or so of plodding along in the business world you won't have much heart to “get serious about something." 

Business is a sort of game-seeing how far you can go, reveling in your financial and moral independence, rising in your own estimation as you improve your general poise and efficiency and mental snap. 502

Women in Business 

Business 

Assets 

HAVING decided upon a business career, you must frankly sum up your assetstraining, ability, sincerity of purpose, charm, beauty, and humor. These are all assets—some of them, alas, rather difficult ones to handle in the business world, but in the long run you will get farther with them than without them. 

Your personal appearance is very important. Remember that before the person from whom you are seeking a position hears your voice, before you even have the time or the opportunity to open your mouth to convince him that you are just the person that he should employ, he has noted your appearance. Your taste in clothes and the way you wear them, their appropriateness and their neatness, are all indications to him of personality. How many a business woman with unusual ability never actually gets an opportunity to show it because of her careless appearance!

There are certain types of clothing that are appropriate for business, and certain others that are not. A woman is daily, hourly, selling herself to the world. And the old world is sometimes so very indifferent. A business woman who dresses inappropriately for her work, no matter how efficient she may be, shows a lack of good taste and a certain lack of character that is bound to have a poor effect upon her employer and business associates. 

You can be well groomed for your work without spending all of your salary trying to keep up with the current mode. It is neatness and efficiency in your clothing that counts. And by "efficiency" in business clothing I mean 503

Lovely Ladies dispensing with frills, ruffles, pleats, bangles, and doodads that hamper, catch in the typewriter keys and knock over the ink; apart from all that, such clothing requires such a great deal of attention, cleaning and pressing, that the upkeep is usually all out of proportion with your income. 

Please realize that neat and efficient business clothes need not entail any sacrifice of your loveliness. They can incorporate the lines and colors that are becoming to you, and they should.

No matter what your ability, when the time comes for a promotion in your office it will be the correctly and quietly dressed girl who will get it.

It has always seemed to me an admission when I see an overdressed girl or woman in the business world—a rather pathetic little admission that she has no social or personal life whatsoever, and that she must, therefore, take this opportunity to show herself off at her prettiest, though not her best.

Don't do it! Your business clothing can be every bit as attractive, every bit as modish, every bit as becoming to you as any of your other clothing, and yet remain within the bounds of sensible good taste. 

Types of Busi ness Clothing

BEFORE we end this chapter let's have a few ideas on what is the correct type of business clothing. Remember, always select lines and colors that are becoming to you! 

Office work requires a different type of clothing than 504

Women in Business does department store work, and work in a large office where you are one of many other employees requires a different type of clothing from work in an office in which you alone are employed. But generally speaking, all office work requires the type of clothing known as sports wear, or tailored wear. In the exceptional case of the office requiring social as well as business contacts, one can wear slightly more elaborate but never fussy afternoon wear.

The business woman can get along very well with but two costumes a year. Of course this depends upon the weather conditions. It does not require a large expenditure of money to be well dressed in business, but careful selection and good taste.

Women who are employed in or about the style industry -department stores, manufacturer's showrooms, and the like-are usually expected to wear very modish dark dresses. How she is to go about this on the salary that she is making is not considered. But a lovely lady can master this circumstance, too, because she will buy a very good conservative model that is extremely becoming to her in line and color, and then will take very good care of it, and wear it for many, many seasons. Which will give her just that much more opportunity to indulge her desire for feminine frills in her other clothes!

It is that last wielding of the whisk broom that will tell the story! Or that little snip at frayed shoe laces with the scissors. Or that quick daub of shoe polish. Just such little things will make all the difference between your being a 505

Lovely Ladies really efficient business woman and a lovely lady, and one who is neither. And you would be astonished if you knew what a psychological effect such things have, not only upon you, but upon your employer and the business world in general.

Summer and the Business Woman 

I BELIEVE that the business woman has an individual summer problem that cannot be compared to anyone's else. She is faced with the need of looking and keeping neat and cool and efficient no matter how much and how often her employer wheezes and puffs and lounges around in his shirt sleeves and says, "Whew, but it's hot to-day!"

He may expect you to agree with him, but he doesn't expect you to look or act the part, and will be annoyed with you if you do.

There is one thing that the clever woman will begin to do in the springtime, and this is to start cutting out of her menus all of the sweets, fats, and starches that she possibly can, and thinning her blood by eating an abundance of fruit and drinking fruit juices. Then in good time she will select her summer business wardrobe for its coolness and its practicability, as well as for its appropriateness to her type of work.

White is undoubtedly the most attractive and practical color to select in dresses that launder, of course, but if you work in an office white will not be as pleasing as you think. 

Instead, the neutral beiges, grays, and pastel colors will be more suitable. 506

Women in Business 

Effective Colors for 

Summer 

THERE are certain colors that give an appearance of heat, and certain others that are cool. Even though you yourself may be as cool as the proverbial cucumber, if you don't give an appearance of coolness to those around you your presence is likely to cause a subconscious if not an actually conscious irritation. 

And the red, brick, henna, and orange shades should be avoided. 

Black, brown, and navy blue are generally to be avoided unless darker colors are more appropriate to your type of work than light ones. These colors in smart crêpe frocks may be relieved by refreshing touches of white or other light shades. Generally speaking, the most inconspicuous and conservative colors are good taste in an office, so this precludes printed things and floral designs of any type.

Appropriate Materials 

BEYOND a doubt the most practical and attractive material for office clothing is crêpe either crêpe de Chine or any one of the flat washable crêpes. It is only possible to wear a summer dress one day or two in an office, and in consequence it must be a color and weight that will stand frequent laundering. Particularly if you launder these dresses yourself is crêpe de Chine excellent, because it is easy to handle and does not require starching. 

Georgettes and chiffons are quite all right for summer business clothes, particularly if they be of the heavier variety, but they require extremely dainty lingerie-and 507

Lovely Ladies 

I personally prefer tailored lingerie for daytime frocks and a lacy variety for afternoon and evening wear. 

Certain cotton materials are ideal. Linen has always been a desirable fabric, but it had a way of crushing easily that was not desirable, so the clever weavers proceeded to make a linen that is uncrushable. This makes linen now extremely good for business dresses. And then there are voiles that have all the charm of silk and all the practicability of cotton.

Details to be considered in selecting a business wardrobe for the summer are: first, good taste in color, in material, and in appropriateness for your type of work; second, its practicability in the matter of laundering and cleaning; third, its "summery” qualities.

What is more disconsolate than a wilted ruffle or a depleated pleat! In an office full of women who start the day with lovely fresh things, evening will see the simple and more tailored frocks every bit as lovely and fresh, but the more ornamental ones wrinkled and bedraggled beyond recognition. Tailored

Lingerie 

THE well-groomed business woman will wear tailored lingerie because it is practical, more easily cared for, cool, and certainly efficient.

Costume slips are very important. Unless one has a costume slip which matches the gown and is attached to it at the shoulders, tailored costume slips fastened to all one's straps with lingerie clasps are the best. There are 508

Women in Business some flat, crinkly crêpes that launder beautifully and do not have to be ironed that make the best-ever costume slips.

What would a business woman's minimum summer wardrobe be? Well, I should say three pairs of light fleshcolored, beige, or gray stockings; two pairs of sports shoes, known as dressy sports shoes, with medium high heelsone dark and one light pair; four chic dresses that have all the desirable qualities mentioned elsewhere; two sports hats; a light-weight summer coat and perhaps a sports jacket; two pairs of washable sports gloves; a bag large enough to carry in it everything you might need at the office and a book to read on the street car; two tailored washable costume slips, and tailored lingerie. 

Understand that this is a minimum wardrobe—the least with which one could possibly get along for the summer without expending too much effort on keeping well groomed. You can add to this minimum wardrobe as much as you wish. A consciousness of being well groomed leaves one's mind and imagination free to go on with the business at hand. To know that you are not well groomed for your position invariably robs you of your poise. 

And Your

Point of View 

Now, having secured the position you wish, and being sure that you know how to dress for the business world, you must also be sure that you are mentally prepared for this business world.

A woman must realize that she is in business for a serious 509

Lovely Ladies purpose, and that as a part of the business world she must submerge herself entirely during business hours, although this does not necessarily mean submerging your personality. But you must banish to a place of secondary importance all of your personal affairs. And you must forget your sex. There are times in life when there are neither men nor women, only intellects. There is absolutely no difference between a male and a female intellect. 

When the office door closes behind you in the morning shelve your family troubles, the party of the night before, the baby's colic, the latest scandal, all your worries about not having received a letter from your sweetheart. If you are not able to do this you can never be a successful business woman.

If you are going to be in business at all, work at it wholeheartedly-dress to it, think of it, and act it 100 per cent.—and leave it just as heartily when the day is done! No lovely lady will carry her business into her home. "My boss said," "the boss," "the boss's wife," are all expressions that can't help but grate upon the ears of those around you. It's all a matter of not losing your perspective. Just try to remember that no one is interested in "the boss" but yourself. 

And now, if you don't like to call yourself a "business woman," call yourself a "lovely lady of the business world!" 510

Chapter VIII THE LOVELY LADY TRAVELS 

THE lovely lady travels and she enjoys herself. And everyone who travels with her enjoys traveling, too. 

Why? Well, principally because she is a lovely lady, of course, but also because she always seems to have everything, do everything, and know everything that is to be had, done, or known about traveling. 

And then one is surprised to learn that no, she has not traveled so very much. How, then, is it that everything about her is in the best possible taste for traveling; that everything she does is appropriate to traveling; and that she seems to be able to get the most fun with the least fuss of anyone else on the voyage? 

I must tell you how she does it! 511

Lovely Ladies General Travel Preparations 

You are going on a journey. Even if this journey is only twenty-four hours long, you have to do more in anticipation of it than just buying your ticket. You must take into consideration: first, your luggage; then, the climate, not only of the place from which you embark but of the place to which you are going. And, of course, there is a great deal of difference between preparing for train travel and preparing for a sea voyage, as well as a great deal of difference between a short five-or six-day journey across the Atlantic and a sixteen-day trip on water via the Panama Canal.

In the matter of luggage (remember we are speaking of general traveling now) a lovely lady will never be without her completely appointed dressing case. Whether this dressing case is elaborately fitted with monogramed toilet articles or is just a simple little box, it will nevertheless serve its purpose. And its purpose is to save you from scrambling through your large suitcase for things that you will need so often during the day-comb, brush, face powder, and things like tha..

And since you should use this well-appointed dressing case for any traveling that you do, let's see what you will need in it.

Comb 

Military brushes Toothbrush

Flat hand mirror 

Nailbrush

Cleansing tissue 

Night skin food 

Hair nets (whether you ordinarily use them or not) 

Nail polish 

Orange stick

Bit of absorbent cotton 512

The Lovely Lady Travels Face powder 

Talcum powder 

Extra powder puff 

Wee box containing four or five needles wrapped with the different colored threads that you might need 

Tiny tin box of matches 

Box of assorted pins and safety pins 

Hand lotion 

Small clothes brush or whisk broom 

Face cloth or sponge 

Cleansing cream

Toothpaste 

Vanishing cream 

Eye wash (important) 

Eye cup Hairpins 

Manicure scissors
